Summary

Post Hawkeye Kate is only 14, her mom just got arrested, and her world just got turned upside down. Now she is headed to the middle of nowhere Iowa on Christmas Eve to spend the holidays with two Avengers and a retired SHIELD agent.

Basically, what would've happened after Hawkeye if Kate was younger and Natasha was still alive?

Notes

I have been debating making this fanfic for a while as I have had the thought of this story for a loooooong time lol. Also bear with me as this is my first fic and I am dyslexic lol. Also for the purposes of this fic, the events of Black Widow have not yet occurred, and Yelena was NOT the Widow that Eleanor hired to take out Clint.

✓♥︎

Malen'kiy Yastreb

    Chapter 1

    New Beginnings Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Sitting on the back of the ambulance, Kate stares off into the distance. The outside noises and her surroundings become a blur as her mind whirls with a million questions.

    What did I just do? She is going to kill me. Will she ever forgive me? Do I really care if she does? Where am I gonna live? Can I move into Dad’s old apartment? I can’t afford that. I can’t afford anything. Am I old enough to get a job? What about school?

    As Kate continued to spiral, the two Avengers approached her.

    “Hey Kate, we should be all set to go! We got some big Christmas plans to get started on! Are you sure you don’t want to get checked out at the hospital? That bruise is starting to look angry,” Clint asks.

    Natasha notices, however, that the young teen had not heard a word her partner said. She slowly kneels down, as to not spook her, and tries to get her attention, “Kate? Are you ok?” Kate flinches slightly and shakes her head to clear her thoughts, “What? Oh yeah, I’m fine. What’s up?”

    “Clint just asked if you are ready to get out of here. And if you are sure that you don’t want to get checked out at the ER.”

    “Oh no!” Kate says quickly, “I’m fine. Totally. Just probably need some sleep is all.”

    Both Natasha and Clint look at her skeptically. Natasha was pretty sure she heard the paramedic say something about broken ribs and possible concussion.

    “Well, if your sure, let's get a move on then Katie-Kate! Christmas fun awaits!”

    After stopping by the safe house to grab Clint and Natasha’s belongings, as well as Lucky, they head to the penthouse to let Kate grab her things.

    As they walk in, Kate feels a pit form in her stomach. There are so many conflicting emotions with the absence of her mom in the house, as well as the thought of Natasha and Clint leaving.

    “Ok, well, thanks for all your guys’ help and for driving me home,” Kate says softly, “I promise to stay out of trouble and to not do any more vigilante-like tasks until I am at least 18. And I am so so sorry again for disturbing your Christmas plans.”

    “First of all, there is nothing to be sorry about, if Clint destroyed that suit when he said he did, none of us would’ve been in this situation in the first place-" Natasha starts.

    “Hey!” Clint protests.

    “Second of all, we all know that you couldn’t last til 18. And third of all, we are just here for you to pack a bag; you’re coming with us kid,” Natasha states.

    “Wait, what?” Kate asks, dumbfounded.

    “Did you really think that we would just leave you here after all we have been through?”

    Natasha asks, chuckling slightly.

    “Well, I… I don’t know,” Kate looks down, sheepishly.

    “Plus, I think both Maria and Fury are already pissed that we let a five-year-old fight with us, they would KILL us if they found out that we just left you here by yourself,” Clint says, “especially since you're injured.”

    Kate rolls her eyes.

    “How many times do I have to tell you I am 14?!”

    “Hmmm, I don't know if I believe you, short-stack. I saw you watching cartoons this morning!”

    “Anyone can watch cartoons, old man! AND you’re the one who put them on!”

    “Yeah, so, but you were watching them!!”

    “Alright, enough you two. Kate, go pack your bag with a few days’ worth of clothes. Clint, stop being annoying,” Natasha sighs, shaking her head at their antics.

    Kate then heads up the stairs to pack her duffle while Clint calls Laura to tell her they are on their way. Natasha then starts to take a look around the penthouse.

    As she wanders, Natasha notices that there are not a lot of pictures of the family on the walls. Just a few of the whole family that were obviously taken by a professional before Derek Bishop died. The whole house screams New York Elite, all fine lines and pristine furniture that barely looks lived in. Everything just seems so clinical and performative. Walking into the living room, she wonders if they even use this room or if its all for show. The back wall is a trophy case that is adorned with every type of trophy you could think of. Natasha wonders if this is also part of the performance, if Kate was also apart of the performance.

    Something about Eleanor Bishop rubbed Natasha the wrong way from the moment she met her. She seemed very cold and almost dictator-like to Kate. And Kate, herself, acted so differently around Eleanor than she did when she was with Clint and Natasha. She was more drawn into herself, and almost seemed scared of the older Bishop.

    Before she can investigate more, Kate and Lucky come down the stairs.

    “Okey dokey,” Clint claps, “Let’s get this show on the road people!”

    Around 2:30 a.m., they finally make it to the farm.

    Kate jolts awake when the Quinjet lands. Blinking, she lets her eyes adjust to the cockpit, and she finds herself a little disappointed that she was asleep for the whole ride and didn’t really get to witness the thrill of riding in the Quinjet.

    “Home sweet home!” Clint sighs, “Come on Katie-Kate!”

    Kate gets her duffle and tugs on Lucky’s leash as they walk up to the house. Clint lets them in and hugs a brunette woman, who Kate guesses is his wife. While his wife hugs Natasha, Kate takes a minute and looks around.

    The house is lit by the Christmas tree and a small fire in the fireplace. This place instantly feels more welcoming and cozier than the penthouse ever has.

    It feels so much warmer.

    “I hope you don’t mind but we brought home some more strays,” Clint says to Laura, “This is Kate and this is Lucky.”

    “Welcome Kate! I’m Laura! Thank you for keeping these two out of trouble,” Laura says as she hugs Kate.

    “Oh, Hi! Um, yeah, I don’t know if I kept them out of trouble or if I pulled them into it,” Kate laughs nervously as the hug ends.

    “Oh, believe me, if I know these two, you definitely kept them out of even more trouble,” Laura laughs, “I am just glad that you all made it home in one piece.” She relaxes while looking at the trio, seeing slight bruising and a few cuts but no major injuries, “I’m sure you are all ready for bed.”

    “You have NO idea, babe,” Clint groans.

    “Kate, you can stay in my room tonight, I can stay on the couch,” Natasha says.

    “No, I don’t want to take away your bed. I have already taken up too much of your holiday; I will be fine on the couch,” Kate insists.

    “No seriously Kate, its totally fine. I want you to. Plus, I need to help these two set up for tomorrow morning.”

    “Yeah, and who knows what time the kids will be up looking for presents tomorrow; you need your rest Katie-Kate,” Clint adds.

    “Ok fine. But tomorrow night, I will be on the couch,” Kate relents, mostly because she is way too tired to argue.

    Natasha shows Kate the way up to her bedroom. Kate looks around in awe of the many pictures on the walls.

    They all seem so happy in them.

    When they reach the end of the hall, Natasha opens the door and lets Kate in. As she walks in, Kate is hit with the smell of vanilla and even more warmth. There are more pictures on the walls in here as well as some art projects no doubt made by the Barton children.

    “Ok, so the bathroom is through that door, feel free to use anything in there. I have an extra blanket at the end of the bed in case you get cold. And yeah, that’s pretty much it. If you need anything, I will be downstairs on the couch. Don’t hesitate to come get me,” Natasha ends with a soft smile.

    “Thank you, Natasha. For everything.” Kate returns her soft smile.

    “Good night, malen’kiy yastreb.”

    Once Natasha leaves, Kate goes and washes up in the bathroom then gets changed in her pajamas she packed. Lucky instantly makes himself at home in the middle of the bed.

    She sits on the bed suddenly finding herself restless. When she was downstairs, she felt like she was going to fall asleep standing up. But now, more of those thoughts and questions she had tried to push away earlier are starting to run through her mind.

    Frustrated, she huffs, then flops down on the bed; instantly regretting that when her ribs and head scream in protest.

    Maybe I should have went to the ER She sighs again as she rolls on her side to pat and snuggle with Lucky. After a while, she finds herself finally caving into a restless sleep.

    Meanwhile, downstairs Laura and Clint are stuffing the children’s stockings as Natasha sits on the couch watching them, nursing a drink.

    “Kate seems so sweet! I am glad you guys found her,” Laura says while taking a bite out of a cookie Nate left out for Santa.

    “Yeah, she is a good kid. It sucks that her mother pulled her into all this shit,” Clint replies sadly.

    “Well did you see their house? It seems like her mother cared more about appearances than actual love. Their living room didn’t even look lived in,” Natasha adds, “And throughout the whole week and at the Christmas Party, she didn’t seem concerned at all for Kate’s safety, she just seemed concerned for her business and public image.”

    “That’s terrible!” Laura says, stopping her actions, looking appalled, “I’m glad you two were there then to watch her.”

    “Yeah, something about that woman doesn’t scream 'Mother of the Year' to me,” Clint says while pouring himself a drink.

    "What happens to her now? I mean I know that her mom was just arrested, but something tells me that her mom hasn’t even thought about child care,” Laura asks.

    “When we found out yesterday how involved Eleanor was, I realized how this might end. So, I started talking with Social Services and Jen Walters,” Natasha says. Clint and Laura look slightly taken aback at the mention of the SheHulk lawyer. “In talking with them, we all figured that the best solution was for Kate to be with people that know her and will take care of her. But, she has no other living family. So…I kinda got myself certified as an emergency foster placement for Kate.”

    Clint and Laura both have expressions mixed of surprise, relief, and a little bit of happiness. “Really? That's great Nat!” Clint smiles, “you and I both know how bad the foster system can be.”

    “I think that you are gonna be great for her Nat!” Laura asks, “did you tell her?”

    “No, not yet. Tonight has been so crazy. She was basically asleep on her feet when she walked in the door. I am gonna talk to her tomorrow,” Nat replies.

    The rest of the night is filled with wrapping, stocking stuffing, drinking, and sugar cookie eating. The adults all file into bed around 4:00 a.m. knowing that in just a few short hours, more craziness will ensue.

    She knew that she probably had a concussion but she also did not have a restful sleep which added to her headache.

    Looking at the alarm clock on the bedside table, she groans internally to find that it is only 8:07 a.m. She debates going back to sleep when a small whine catches her attention. Lucky is at the door wagging his tail hoping to be let out of the room. It is then when she realizes it is Christmas morning, and she should probably get up.

    So, despite her exhaustion, she makes the decision to get out of bed. Sitting up, Kate curses under her breath as she REALLY regrets not going to the hospital the night before.

    At least they would’ve given me some type of pain meds.

    She slowly makes her way to the door to go downstairs, but before she turns the knob, she pauses.

    Listening to all the joy downstairs.

    Christmas at her house never light sounded like that. It was never merry. It was stuffy dinners with cranky rich people, uncomfortable dresses that her mother made her wear, and over-the- top gifts from her mother that she didn’t want but would seem impressive to the guests they had.

    Shaking her head to clear her thoughts, she opens the door and slowly makes her way down to the source of the noise.

    Lucky runs past her, almost knocking her over in the process.

    “Easy Pizza Dog!” Kate tells him as she tries to right herself and he barrels into the living room.

    "Oooo! A puppy! Is he a part of our Christmas gifts?!” Nate squeals.

    “Sorry, Nate, that’s not our dog. That’s Lucky! He is Kate’s dog,” Clint replies, looking up to see Kate entering the room, “Speak of the devil. Good morning, Katie-Kate! Hope we didn’t wake you up.”

    “Oh no, it’s all good,” Kate said, smiling softly.

    “Well come in and sit, and we will introduce you to everyone!”

    Kate walks more into the room and sees Laura sitting in a chair near the tree, Clint sitting on the floor with his younger son and daughter, his older son sitting on a love seat opposite the fireplace and the tree, and Natasha sitting on the couch with a mug in her hands. Natasha sends her a soft smile and pats the seat next to her and Kate sits down.

    “So Kate, this is Nate! He is 6,” he says pointing to the boy in front of him who is currently too distracted by Lucky to respond, “this is Lila! She is 14 just like you!” as he points to her, Lila smiles and waves to her, “and that over there is Cooper! He is 16.”

    “Nice to meet you, Kate,” Cooper greets her.

    “Hi! Its nice to meet all you guys. Sorry I kept your dad and your aunt away so long this Christmas,” Kate apologizes.

    “Don’t worry about it,” Cooper says, “Knowing my dad, it was probably all his fault anyway —”

    “Hey!” Clint cuts in.

    “Besides,” Lila adds, “you got him home in time for Christmas anyways. Plus, he brought you here! And you seem wicked cool! I mean, running into traffic to save a dog?! AND you like archery?! We are basically already on track to being besties! I like archery too, but it’s more of a hobby. But Dad said you’re pretty good, so that probably means your better than him.”

    “Well thank you,” Kate laughs, “And yes, I don’t know if I am a better shot than him but I am definitely as good as him.”

    “Wow dad, if Kate is as good as you are right now, she will definitely end up being a way better Hawkeye than you,” Cooper laughs.

    Clint just groans.

    As the kids were opening up more presents, Natasha looks over at Kate. After watching her for a while, Natasha notices that, despite sleeping later than everyone else, Kate still seems exhausted. And all of her actions, as well as her breathing, seem very stiff and as if she is in pain.

    “How did you sleep, Kate?” Natasha asks her while everyone else is distracted by Nate opening his new LEGO set.

    “Oh, ya know… okay, I guess. I think I am a little sore from yesterday though so… yeah,”

    Kate finishes lamely.

    Natasha can tell than she is lying and wonders if the teen got any sleep at all last night, but she decides not to push Kate on the issue.

    “How sore are you? Do you want some ibuprofen? I think you should probably take some,”

    Natasha says.

    “Nothing too bad. But some ibuprofen wouldn’t hurt,” Kate shrugs.

    Natasha, again, can tell she is lying; but again, does not call Kate out on it. Instead, she gets up and returns with the ibuprofen bottle. She frowns slightly at the sight of Kate taking four of the pills instead of the recommended two.

    “You can take another dose in about 6 hours,” Natasha states while thinking that they should probably find something a little stronger for her.

    “Thank you.”

    The rest of the morning is filled with exchanging presents and pleasant conversation. Kate had felt like she was slightly intruding, but the Barton’s slowly made her feel more and more welcome.

    Once all the presents were handed out, and after breakfast, the family returned to the living room to relax for a while. When Laura enters the room, armed with hot chocolates, and she announces that it is movie time.

    “I think we should let Kate pick the first movie since this is her first Christmas with us,” Laura says.

    “But I wanna pick!” Nate pouts.

    “No, let’s let Kate pick, I don’t wanna watch Elf for the billionth time this week, and I KNOW that’s the one you are gonna pick,” Lila states, matter-of-factly. The adults just laugh. “I mean I don’t have to pick, its okay if Nate wants to,” Kate adds quickly.

    “No, its okay Kate, I honestly don’t think I can watch Elf again this year and Nate can pick after; so its all you!” Clint says.

    Kate ends up picking Christmas Vacation, a classic movie that everyone should watch at least once in a Christmas season, but Kate finds it hard to pay attention as her exhaustion starts to win over. With finally having some sort of pain relief in her system, she loses the last of her fight to stay awake.

    About fifteen minutes into the movie, Natasha looks over to see Kate cuddled up in the corner of the couch, fast asleep, with her empty mug slowly slipping out of her grasp.

    Smiling softly, she leans over to take the mug, then pulls the blanket off the back of the couch to drape it over Kate.

    Kate wakes up to see the Griswold Family singing the national anthem at the end of the movie, feeling much more rested than she had previously.

    When the credits roll, Clint looks up and over to Natasha, then to her and says, “Hey Kate, could you help me with something real quick before the next movie?”

    "Yeah, sure,” Kate replies and follows him and Natasha as they leave the living room and head to the side door; stopping to put on boots and jackets, the trio heads outside.

    Once outside, Clint walks over to a grill and sets his Ronin suit on it.

    “I figured you’d like to help me end what you started,” Clint says while dousing the suit in lighter fluid.

    “What she started?” Natasha asks with a raised eyebrow and a smirk.

    “Okay fine, what I started, then thought I ended, then she inadvertently re-started, which shouldn’t have happened because I should have done this a while ago, THEN you both helped me end. Happy?” Clint said, looking at Natasha while handing Kate a book of matches.

    Natasha chuckles.

    “I may be concussed, and running on no sleep, but that still made absolutely no sense,” Kate says striking a match, then tossing it into the grill, causing the suit to go up in flames. After a while of watching the suit burn, Kate looks at Clint, “Can I ask you something?”

    “Yeah, sure.”

    “What do you think of “’Lady Hawk”?’ Kate smirks.

    “Oh, that’s terrible.” Clint says as they start walking back towards the house.

    “’Hawk Eve”?’

    “No, that’s worse,” Clint replies. Natasha just shakes her head with a fond smirk.

    “Damn, ok. “Hawk Shot?” Like “hot shot.” But you know, “Hawk”’

    “No”

    “’Lady Arrow”?’

    “None of those”

    “Ok fine,” Kate says defeatedly.

    “You know what actually I have an idea!” Clint says like he has had this thought the entire time, “it’s a pretty great name and I know for a fact that it will make a good impact.”

    “What? What is it?” Kate asks. Natasha smirks at Clint because she already knows where he is going with this.

    “Hawkeye” Clint states plainly, smiling at the look of excitement on Kate’s face, “We can share the name. I am basically retired and plus there is no one else I would rather take over for me. HOWEVER, you are not allowed to fully become/use the Hawkeye mantle until you are at least 18 and have had enough training from me and Nat.”

    Which earns a groan from Kate and a chuckle from Natasha.

    The rest of the day is nothing but relaxing and, is frankly, the best Christmas Kate can remember since her dad passed. It was filled with classic movies, intense Mario Kart tournaments, the best hot chocolate Kate had ever tasted, even more delicious food, and enough laughter to make her sides hurt.

    She definitely felt a lot happier than she had this morning.

    Currently, Natasha, Clint, Kate, and Laura were sitting in the living room watching some Christmas special on TV, as the Barton kids headed to bed earlier.

    Kate found herself barely able to keep her eyes open.

    “Alright, I think I am gonna head to bed,” Clint stands up and announces, “I can’t wait to finally sleep in tomorrow.” Laura agrees, and they both say their goodnights and go upstairs; which leaves Kate and Natasha alone.

    Looking over at Kate, Natasha can’t help but notice how much more grown up the girl looks since she first met her. Something about her reminded Natasha of herself. The whole day, the teen had been quieter than she had been the whole previous week.

    “So, how are you feeling kid?” Natasha asks, “I know that these past few days have been super overwhelming,” she sees Kate hesitate so she adds, “especially a with a Barton Christmas tacked on the end,” bumping her shoulder lightly into Kate’s.

    “Heh… okay I guess,” Kate chuckles softly and looks down at her hands, “I will admit that I feel a lot better now than I did this morning, but everything still feels uncertain.” She looks up at Natasha, “I honestly don’t really know what to feel just yet, and I am not really ready to unpack all that, but right now I am more concerned with what comes next, I guess,” she shrugs.

    "Well, whenever you are ready to talk, you are welcome to come and talk to me. And as for what comes next,” Natasha says slowly, “I have talked to the social worker assigned to your case.”

    As soon as she says this, she sees Kate’s face shift into panic.

    “Social worker? Oh ok, that makes sense I guess,” Kate replies nervously.

    “Woah wait let me finish kid,” Natasha chuckles, “In talking with them and a lawyer, we all thought that the best course of action would be setting you up with someone that you already know and that will take care of you. So, I applied and became certified as your emergency foster parent… if that’s okay with you?”

    “Really?” Kate asks in disbelief, “You want to take me in? After all the shit I put you and Clint through? Why?”

    “How many times do I have to tell you that it wasn’t your fault?” she laughs with fond exasperation, “As for your other question, I don’t know. I see a part of myself in you… And believe it or not, I am starting to get kinda used to having you around,” she winks.

    Kate looks up at Natasha, with so much gratitude and hope that it might just break her.

    “Thank you, Nat.”

    Natasha reaches out and squeezes Kate’s shoulder as she gets up from the couch. “Now, get some rest kid. We got a long drive back to the Compound tomorrow.”

    “Ok,” Kate replies, getting herself comfy on the couch, “Goodnight Natasha. Thank you.”

    “Goodnight, malen’kiy yastreb.”

    Before realizes, Kate is fast asleep, finally resting more soundly than she did the night before. Chapter End Notes Here's chapter two!     To the Compound Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Despite getting a more restful sleep, morning still came too soon for Kate when she woke up from the sounds of the adults making coffee. After a quick breakfast, Natasha and Kate packed their things, and Lucky, into Nat’s corvette. They said their goodbyes to each of the Barton children, promising to come visit soon, and Kate exchanging phone numbers with Lila.

    “Okay, I think that’s everything,” Natasha says hugging Clint, “We will see you in a week at Stark’s New Years Party?”

    “Yep! Although, I think it will just be me and Laura since the kids have school on the 2nd ,” Clint replies.

    “Now that you have been here, you are welcome at anytime Kate. This is your home now too, ok?” Laura says giving Kate as hug.

    “Thank you, Laura!” Kate says as she returns the warm hug. Laura sends her a soft smile, then goes to go say goodbye to Natasha.

    “Okay Hawkeye, promise me you won’t go knocking down anymore iconic trees, and be good for Tasha, huh?” Clint says, ruffling Kate’s hair. She laughs and knocks his hand off her head. “Seriously kid, I meant what I said that day. I am really proud of you. And if you ever need anything, you let me know, alright?”

    “Thank you, Clint, for… well, for everything,” Kate looks up with a soft smile. Clint pulls her into a hug.

    Natasha, Kate, and Lucky pile into the car and start their way down the old farm road on their journey to the Compound.

    Their trip takes most of the day, they stop every few hours to let Lucky stretch his legs and to grab food.

    Once they hit Pennsylvania, Natasha is surprised that Kate has not fallen asleep at all during the drive. She was expecting to have to nudge Kate every few hours to make sure she takes the ibuprofen that she has been taking religiously every chance she could get. This morning, She noticed the fatigue on Kate’s face when they were packing the car. It had been a long week for her; hell, it’s been a long week for the both of them, but Kate seemed determined to stay awake on their trip.

    “So, you seem really interested in the scenery, huh kid?” Natasha asks.

    “Oh, um, yeah I guess,” Kate blushes.

    “I just thought that you would’ve taken this time to rest, I am guessing that you probably haven’t slept well since this all started,” Natasha states casually, even though she knows for a fact that Kate hasn’t been sleeping well.

    “Oh, well, I just have never really been on a road trip before. I actually haven’t been outside of New York since my dad died,” Kate says, completely ignoring the second half of Natasha's comment as she continues to look out the window.

    Natasha frowns.

    This is her first time outside of New York in over 7 years? Wasn’t her family rich? Has Eleanor never taken Kate on vacation anywhere? Not even just for “appearance” sake?

    She shakes her head to clear her thoughts.

    “I get it. When I first defected to SHIELD, I made Clint drive everywhere. I loved to watch how the landscape changes,” she sends Kate a small smile. “When things settle down, maybe we can take a road trip out to visit the Farm again? Take our time on our way?”

    “Really?”

    “Yeah of course! How do you think my car got there?” Natasha replies causing both her and Kate to chuckle. “It would be fun.”

    “Yeah, it would,” Kate replies, and they fall back into a comfortable silence listening to the soft rock on the radio.

    By the time they cross the border into New York, Kate had succumbed to her exhaustion.

    Natasha doesn’t blame her and is glad that the teen is getting some much-needed rest.

    They finally pull up to the Compound around 9:30 p.m.

    As the car is put in park, Kate jerks awake. Natasha grabs their bags, as Kate grabs Lucky’s leash. She follows Natasha down a few hallways that she is too tired to remember before they get to Natasha’s apartment. In contrast to the clean cut and high-tech appearance of the rest of the building thus far, Natasha’s apartment is much brighter and more relaxing.

    Walking in, Kate gets hit with the same smell of vanilla from Natasha's room at the Barton’s ranch, but here, it is somehow more calming.

    “So, here we are, home sweet home,” Natasha gestures about the room.

    Its an open-concepted living space with a good-sized kitchen connected to the living room that has a large sectional, a chair, and a flat screen TV.

    “Here, I will show you around really quick.”

    She shows Kate where everything is in the kitchen, with a promise that she is allowed anything at any time, she shows Kate her office that she claims she doesn’t use, and then she shows Kate her new bedroom.

    As Kate walks in, she finds that it has the same homey feeling the rest of the apartment does. “This is your room; I know its bare right now but we can go back and get some more of your things tomorrow. We can also go shopping for some decorations and other things you will need,” Natasha says.

    The room has a full-size bed, a desk, a bureau, and a TV mounted on the wall.

    “Ok, thank you,” Kate sends her a small smile.

    “There is an attached bathroom through that door, and oh hold on one second, I will be right back,” Natasha leaves the room and comes back a few seconds later with a few things in her hand. “Here is a towel, in case you want to shower, there should be products in bathroom already. Also, here is a hoodie. Sometimes during the winter, especially if I haven’t been here for a while, the heat takes a bit to kick on.”

    She hands Kate the towel and sweatshirt.

    “Thank you,” Kate says, taking the stuff from Natasha, looking down at her hands “for everything.”

    “No problem, kid. But you don't need to keep saying 'Thank you.' You have said it so much in the past 48 hours it’s starting not to sound like words anymore,” Natasha chuckles, “Seriously, it’s no big deal.”

    Kate just laughs softly.

    “How are your injuries doing?” Natasha asks, changing the subject. She has a good guess as to what Kate will probably answer with but tries anyway.

    “Oh, they're fine; I’m fine.” Kate lies.

    “Phooey! Last week you took out Kingpin with nothing but broken trick arrows and a cufflink,” Clint says.

    Everyone stops their side conversations and looks over at Kate. The whole room goes silent. She feels her heartrate pick up.

    “Damn, I knew you guys got into some trouble in the city around Christmas time, I didn’t know it was with the Kingpin!” Steve speaks up first.

    “Yeah, and Kate took him down all by herself,” Clint adds proudly, “that’s why she is taking over for me when she turns 18.”

Now his wife, Eleanor I think, runs the company. Still invites me to all the parties and gala’s every year. Used to think she was a nice woman, but—”

    Natasha looks down at Kate as she feels her tense up.

    “Tony, shut up,” Natasha tries to interject, but he keeps barreling on.

    Kate’s heart starts to race faster.

    “But I just saw on the news the other day that she was arrested for mob participation and murder,” Tony continues.

    Kate is slowly starting to tremble as her breathing gets heavier.

    No, no, no, please. Not now. I can’t deal with this right now, please stop.

    “Tony,” Natasha warns again in a harsher tone, glaring at him. She puts her arm around Kate to offer her some support.

    “What did I do?” Tony asks, genuinely confused.

    “Do you need any help?” Wanda is the one that asks her, but Clint and Laura’s eyes hold the same question.

    “No, its okay, thank you though,” Natasha leaves, sending one last final glare to Tony.

    “What did I do?” Tony asks, again, softer.

    Clint lets out a disgruntled sigh and wipes his hand down his face.

    “Kate is Eleanor Bishop’s daughter.”

    “Kate?” Natasha calls, walking into the apartment. Getting no response, she moves to go check her bedroom.

    “Kate?” she calls again as she approaches the room.

    Doctor's Visits and Playing Cupid Chapter Notes TW for allusions to child abuse See the end of the chapter for more notes For the first time since this whole mess started, Kate wakes up calmly and well rested. Sitting up, she clicks open her phone to see that it is 10:30. Damn, I really needed that sleep, huh. She looks around and notices that Lucky is no longer in her room.

    She stands up, and tries to stretch out her stiff muscles but stops when pain rips through her side. Oh shit, right lol, my ribs. It is then she notices voices out in the living room.

    Making her way out into the living room, she finds Natasha, Clint, and Laura sitting on the sectional nursing cups of coffee and talking. Clint notices her first.

    “Good morning Sleeping Beauty!” Kate rolls her eyes at the comment, “We were wondering when you were going to wake up,” Clint sends her a smile.

    Natasha and Laura both turn to look at Kate. Laura sends her a warm smile while Natasha looks at her softly. Kate goes and sits next to Natasha, leaning ever so slightly close to her. Lucky bounds over to her, eager to get some good morning pats from his favorite human.

    “Yeah,” Kate replies, “but I will get some in a little while.”

    Before Kate knew it, it was time for her appointment with Dr. Cho. Following Natasha down to medbay, Kate felt her anxiety increase with every step.

    Its just to get pain meds. She will probably just take x-rays. Nothing major. You can do this. Natasha picks up on her nervousness, “You okay, Kate?”

    “She has just been taking ibuprofen,” Natasha supplies, “and I have been making sure she ices her ribs at least once a day.”

    “Wow, just ibuprofen, you must be in a great deal of pain!” Dr. Cho sends Kate a sympathetic wince, “We will definitely be sending you guys home with something stronger.”

    Just then a nurse comes in to take Kate down to x-ray.

    Once Kate is gone, Dr. Cho turns to Natasha. “Hopefully it is just one broken rib, and it heals quickly. She must’ve been in pain for a while. There is something I wanted to talk to you about before she comes back.”

    Natasha instantly turns her full attention to Dr. Cho.

    “Before her appointment, I requested her medical records and history to look over everything, mostly to make sure she had no allergies or pre-existing conditions.” Dr. Cho pauses, “However, what I found, was quite shocking.”

    She hands Natasha a file.

    Natasha’s eyes widen and her heart drops as she looks through the file. Multiple ER visits starting from a few months after her father passed away, all throughout her childhood, with the most recent one being in November.

    She feels sick to her stomach.

    Sprains, broken bones, stitches, even a concussion.

    Each one claiming clumsiness for the “reason of injury.”

    A white, hot, anger suddenly forms behind Natasha’s eyes.

Good news is they look like they are already in the healing process! So, best course of action will be wrapping your ribs daily in an ace bandage and continuing to ice them as you have been doing. I will also be sending you home with some stronger pain medication as previously discussed. Do you have any questions?” She looks at Natasha and Kate.

    “Thank you, Helen,” Natasha replies.

    The Visit Chapter Notes TW for child abuse See the end of the chapter for more notes A week later, Kate found herself having a hard time falling asleep. It was the night before her scheduled visit with her mother. She tossed and turned for a few hours trying her hardest to shake off the anxiety and fall asleep. When she finally did fall asleep, she wished she would’ve just stayed awake.

    Around 3:00 a.m. Natasha jerked awake when she heard a scream followed by a plea for help.

    “Agent Romanoff, it seems that Miss Bishop is in distress.” FRIDAY announced.

    Yeah, no shit.

    Natasha jumps out of bed and grabs her gun from her night stand. She nearly sprints to Kate’s bedroom, and doesn’t even knock before opening the door. After finding no threats, she notices Kate is thrashing around in bed, mumbling, clearly stuck in a nightmare.

    This isn’t the first time this week she’s had to wake Kate up from a nightmare since the teen found out about her upcoming visit with her mother, but this seems to be the worst one she has had. The poor girl is twisted in her sheets with tears running down her cheeks as she continues to cry out.

    Natasha's heart breaks at the sight.

    She places her gun on Kate’s dresser, she sits on the side of Kate’s bed, and puts her hand on Kate’s shoulder to gently shake her awake.

    “Kate? Wake up dorogoy.”

    “No… no… please stop… I am sorry,” Kate cries, still stuck in her nightmare.

    “It's time to wake up Kate,” Natasha says a little louder as she shakes her a little harder. Suddenly Kate sits up, glancing around the room with unfocused eyes, breathing fast. Part of her still half-asleep and stuck in her dream.

    “Kate? You are okay, malen’kiy yastreb. You are safe,” Natasha says to her calmly.

    Kate finally shifts her focus to Natasha. Once their eyes lock, Kate starts to sob, and throws her arms around Natasha. She is momentarily shocked at the action but immediately feels her heart warm at the fact that Kate feels so safe with her, so Natasha just catches her and shifts to pull Kate fully into her arms. She rubs circles on Kate’s back while rocking, ever so slightly, back and forth.

    “Shhh, its okay dorogoy. You are safe now. You are safe,” she soothes as Kate cries.

    After a few minutes, Kate’s tears slow down, and she slowly pulls away from Natasha.

    Natasha reaches and wipes the stray tears off her cheeks.

    “Are you okay, malen’kiy yastreb?” At this Kate just shrugs.

    Huh, that’s a first. She usually just tells me that she is okay now that she is awake, then she goes back to sleep.

    Because of the shift in routine, Natasha risks, “Do you want to talk about it?”

    Kate looks indecisive, so Natasha adds, “it helps me sometimes. To talk it out.”

    Kate nods, and Natasha makes sure she knows that she has her full attention.

    “It was an old memory.” Kate starts, looking down at her hands, “Last year, I was assigned a group project with some friends at school. My mom was away on a business trip, and I was never allowed to have friends over. So I thought since she was gone, they could come over. And she would never know. I told them they could come over to my house to work on the project. We were halfway done with our presentation when my mother walked through the door. And she was pissed. She kicked all my friends out, even after I told her that we were just working on something for school. She screamed at me. She must’ve been drinking on the plane because I could smell whisky on her breath. Ranting about how I threw a party, which,” Kate pauses and looks up at Natasha, almost desperate for her to know that she wasn't doing anything wrong, before continuing, “I didn’t Tasha, I promise, we were just working on a project.”

    “I believe you dorogoy,” Natasha says sincerely, rubbing Kate’s arm soothingly.

All the while still yelling about how I was a spoiled brat who didn’t deserve everything she did for me. She just kept hitting me… The next morning, she woke up and apologized, saying she didn’t mean it and that it was my fault because I shouldn’t have provoked her when she had been drinking.”

    “Oh Kate,” Natasha pulls Kate back into her arms as she starts crying again, feeling her own eyes tear up. After a few moments, she says, “none of that is your fault.”

    “But—” Kate sniffles.

    “No buts Kate,” Natasha says gently but firmly, “no one should’ve ever put their hands on you no matter the circumstance. Especially your own mother. And I won’t let her or anyone else lay a finger on you again. I promise.”

    Kate hugs her tighter, and they sit in comfortable silence for a moment longer.

    “Dorogoy?” she waits until Kate looks up at her to continue, “the other day in Dr. Cho’s office, she showed me your medical history.”

    Kate tenses up.

    Natasha continues to rub circles on her back to calm her, “Was that every time your mother hit you?”

    “Those where the times that it was bad enough that she had to take me to get checked the next day. Usually when she sobered up,” Kate replies softly, looking down, "She would make me lie and say that I tripped at school or at practice."

    “I will never let her lay her hands on you again,” Natasha repeats, resolutely, hugging Kate tighter.

    After a while, between Natasha’s steady breathing and her warm hand rubbing her back, Kate finds herself unable to keep her eyes open.

    I don’t want to fall back asleep.

    Every time her eyes close for an extended period of time, Kate jerks herself back awake.

    “It’s okay, malen’kiy yastreb. Go back to sleep.”

    But I don’t want you to leave when I do.

    “Tasha?”

    “Yes, milaya?”

    “Will you stay with me?”

    “Of course.”

    Natasha moves to lay down on her back. Kate lays on her side next to her, putting her head on Natasha’s shoulder. Natasha puts her arm around Kate, and before they know it, they are both fast asleep.

    In the morning, Natasha wakes up feeling a weight on her chest. She looks down to see that Kate has shifted in the night to cuddle into her more, her head is now resting on Natasha’s chest and her arms are curled around her torso. Smiling, she relaxes a little while longer before looking over at the alarm clock on Kate’s bedside table, indicating it is 8:15 a.m. Groaning internally, she starts to gently wake Kate, as they need to be at the jail at 10 for their appointment with Eleanor.

    “Come on, Kate, its time to wake up.”

    Then she remembers why they are getting up, and her smile falters.

    “Believe me, I want to stay in bed all day too, but we have things we unfortunately can’t skip today,” she chuckles.

    At this Kate groans again, but reluctantly opens her eyes.

    Realizing her current position, she sits up rather quickly, forgetting about her still healing ribs. Wincing slightly, she looks back to Natasha with a light blush on her cheeks, “Sorry.” “Did anyone ever tell you that you say sorry too much, kid?” Natasha laughs lightly, sending her a soft smile, “It’s okay.”

    “What time is it?” Kate asks.

    “It is 8:20,” Natasha replies, and Kate groans for the third time this morning.

    “Welp, the sooner we do this, the sooner it’s over with, right?” Kate says, trying to quell her growing anxiety.

    On the ride to the prison, Kate could feel her heart rate increasing with every passing minute. By the time they reach the parking lot, Kate is well aware that her breathing has become fast and uneven. And given by the concerned glances Natasha keeps subtly throwing at her, she has no doubt Natasha has noticed. Putting the car in park, Natasha pauses for a minute.

    “I will be there the entire time,” Natasha says softly squeezing her shoulder in reassurance. Kate just nods, trying to bring her breathing back to a normal rate.

    I can do this; it will only be thirty minutes. I am ready. I can do this.

    Taking a big deep breath, Kate turns to look Natasha in the eye.

    “Okay," She takes one last deep breath, "I am ready.”

    As they are lead through the prison back to a room labeled ‘Family Meeting Room,’ Kate’s anxiety grows exponentially. They are shown to a waiting area while the guards go to retrieve Eleanor. Kate’s leg immediately starts to bounce quickly and she reaches out to grab Natasha’s hand, who squeezes it in return.

    “Actually Natasha, Eleanor requested to see Kate alone.”

    Ok. I lied. I am not ready for this.

    “Hi sweetheart,” Eleanor says with a small smile, and draws Kate in to a hug. Despite being in prison for over two weeks, her mother still holds the same confident and professional aura that she has always had.

    And honestly, Kate finds herself a bit impressed.

    “Hi mom,” she replies, softly.

    Looking around the room, it looks like a waiting room at a pediatrician’s office; with various toys and stuffed animals as well as couches.

    Huh, this is not what I thought this room would look like.

    They go to sit on the couch.

    I don’t know, maybe it’s because your business partner decided to throw me into a shelf and break a few of my ribs.

Everything will be back the way it used to be.”

    The next few days followed in similar fashion. Kate would wake up, her and Clint would eat breakfast, then they would go down to the range to practice archery for a while. (However, the day following the lab incident was spent cleaning up the lab while the next was spent replacing all of the arrows they had destroyed). After Clint and Kate were done shooting, Kate would spend some time with Wanda in the library reading. Then the three of them would have dinner, watch tv or a movie, Kate would text Natasha (sometimes receiving texts back while other times not), then head to bed.

    However, as the days went on, Kate was slowly starting to feel more and more anxious at Natasha’s absence.

    She wasn’t getting restful sleep, and her nightmares had started to become more frequent. Clint and Wanda began to notice her increasing exhaustion and overall unease. They tried to do more exciting things to help cheer her up and get her mind off of things. Clint showed her how to hang out in the vents, his small little ‘Hawk Cave’ he created in the vents a few years ago, and taught her some new trick shots when they were down at the range. Wanda taught Kate how to make paprikash, she started to do some yoga with her everyday, and took her out to get ice cream.

    And it definitely helped slightly lower her growing anxiety.

    But by Day 6 of Natasha being gone, nothing was really helping.

    Kate felt her anxiety continue to grow, especially as Natasha stopped responding to her texts the day before.

    Which is okay, right? She said that she wouldn’t be able to respond right away. But she said she would only be gone a few days. It’s been almost a week. Did something happen to her?

    She still went with Clint down to the range, after she ate very few bites of the pancakes he made, but she didn’t want to try any of the trick shots he would suggest, opting to just quietly shoot normally. She didn’t eat her lunch when it was offered to her. Her building anxiety caused her to be rather nauseous. And when she went to the library with Wanda after, Wanda noticed that she wasn’t reading, she was just staring out the window while bouncing her leg up and down anxiously.

    By dinnertime, Clint and Wanda were both getting worried as they watched Kate just push the food around her plate.

    “I think I am full,” Kate says looking down at her plate, not having taken a single bite, “I am going to go take a shower.”

    She excuses herself from the table and goes into her room.

    Clint and Wanda share a concerned glance.

    “She didn’t eat any of her food,” Wanda says, getting up to start cleaning up after dinner. Clint frowns.

    “She barely ate her breakfast and basically refused her lunch. And she hasn't been sleeping well, either,” Clint adds, “I had to wake her up twice last night from nightmares and I am pretty sure she ended up just staying up the rest of the night.”

    Wanda frowns as well, “She has been very quiet today. The only thing we talked about was if I had heard from Natasha recently, which I haven't. I guess she hasn't heard from her since the night before last.”

    “Hopefully she is back soon,” Clint says.

    Just then, the front door opens.

    “Hey guys!” Natasha says walking in.

    “Wow! I should play the lottery!” Clint jokes walking over to hug Natasha, “We were just talking about you.”

    “I hope it was all good things,” Natasha says sending Wanda a wink.

    “It always is,” Wanda replies, “but we were really just talking about Kate.”

    “Oh! Where is she?” Natasha smiles looking around for her, “I have been patiently waiting to watch the next She-ra episodes,” she chuckles.

    “She just went to go take a shower,” Wanda answers, laughing slightly at Natasha’s joke.

    “Ah I see,” Natasha says, “How has she been?” Then Natasha notices a shift in emotion in Clint and Wanda, the smile falling off her face, "What?"

    “She was fine the beginning of this week,” Clint starts, “but I think she has been experiencing some separation anxiety the past few days.”

    Natasha frowns.

    “She hasn’t been sleeping well; you can tell how tired she is,” Wanda adds, “and today… she has been very quiet and didn’t eat very well.”

    Natasha feels her heart break.

    Hopefully after seeing that I am here now, she will start to feel better.

    “Maybe I should wait awhile before going on my next mission,” Natasha says.

    “Honestly Nat,” Clint says, “speaking as someone who is also a parent who has to leave their children for missions—”

    “I am not exactly her—”

    “But you want to be right?” Clint asks, looking at Natasha who pauses before nodding resolutely, “Okay then. As I was saying, it is going to be hard on both of you in the beginning, but soon, she will get more used to it. Trust me.”

    Natasha nods again, then looks up to see Kate walking out of her room in her pajamas.

    Kate instantly brightens when she sees Natasha.

    “Tasha? You’re home?” Kate smiles, as she runs over to give Natasha a tight hug.

    “Yeah kid, I couldn’t leave you and Clint alone for a full week,” Natasha laughs wrapping her arms around Kate, “even with Wanda’s supervision you guys still managed to blow something up.”

    Kate laughs.

    A couple hours later, Kate is soundly asleep with her head on Natasha’s shoulder as they watch a movie. Once the credits roll, she looks over to Clint, who is sitting on the end of the sectional, and Wanda, who is sitting right next to her.

    “Thank you guys for watching her this week,” Natasha says softly.

    “Anytime Nat,” Wanda replies, smiling.

    “Yeah,” Clint adds as he gets stretches, “you have watched my kids so many times, its about time I start repaying the favor,” he chuckles, “I think I am gonna call it a night, see you two in the morning.”

    With that he leaves and Wanda speaks up, “I think I am also going to head out.”

    “Okay,” Natasha says, “We still on for our date tomorrow night?”

    “I wouldn’t miss it for the world,” Wanda replies, giving Natasha a chaste kiss, “Goodnight.” “Goodnight,” Natasha returns with a dreamy smile.

    When she hears the door shut, she looks down at Kate. She slowly shifts Kate into her arms, smiling when Kate cuddles closer to her in her sleep, and carries her to her bedroom. She gently places Kate on the bed and pulls her covers up over her shoulder.

    “Goodnight, malen’kiy yastreb,” she whispers as she leans down and places a soft kiss on the top of Kate’s head.

    The Trial Chapter Notes TW for allusions to child abuse, it is not explicitly talked about but it is a topic of discussion See the end of the chapter for more notes A few days after Natasha had returned home from her mission, she had received a call from Jen Walters saying that they had set a court date for Eleanor; February 1st. She had also mentioned that since they were looking into Eleanor’s parenting ability, that they might be asking for some records from Kate’s medical history and possibly interviewing her. They are hoping to be able to be taking Eleanor up on charges of child abuse as well, but that would be kept under wraps from the press.

    In the week leading up to Eleanor’s trial, Kate felt a low rumbling of anxiety. And as the days drew closer, Kate was getting more and more restless. She felt like she could barely sit still. She was so thankful when Dr. Cho cleared her for some light exercise, and spent most of her days down at the gym. Kate was trying everything to pull her focus away from the trial.

    Her restlessness continued into the night as well. Endless thoughts kept her awake all night; she could barely fall asleep. And when she did, she was faced with more traumatic nightmares about her past. Natasha was getting increasingly concerned by her lack of sleep. The only time Kate seemed to relax and sleep well was when she would be with Natasha, often asking Natasha to stay when she would wake up from a nightmare.

    The morning of the trial, Kate had not slept the entire night, tossing and turning. Around 6:30, she had had enough.

    I just want a little bit of sleep before I have to deal with the shitshow that is today.

    She got out of bed, and after a few minutes of internal debate, made her way over to Natasha’s room.

    She pauses outside of her room.

    She did say her door was always open.

    Shaking herself off, she raises her hand to knock. But she hears something that makes her pause again.

    Natasha is talking to someone.

    Maybe on the phone? No, there is another voice in there.

    Is that…Wanda?!? OMG!

    Kate’s eyes widen, and the voices get closer to the door. Panicking, not knowing what exactly to do, Kate runs back quietly to her room.

    As freaked out as I am right now, I am so happy for them.

    Once she hears Wanda leave, she waits another 15 minutes before going to Natasha’s room.

    This time, she knocks right away, and Natasha opens the door. She immediately notes Kate’s exhaustion.

    “Hi dorogoy, did you get any sleep?” Natasha asks. Kate just shakes her head no. Natasha frowns. “Do you want to go watch some TV in your room for a while? we don’t need to start getting ready til around 9:30.” Kate nods her head, and they both make their way to Kate’s room. Once the TV is on, Kate wastes no time in snuggling up to Natasha, and within minutes, she is fast asleep.

    The whole ride to the courthouse was a blur.

    Kate didn’t pay attention to the conversations that Natasha, Wanda, and Clint where having; even when they were trying to pull her into the conversation. As they were sitting on the bench waiting to be let into the courtroom, she didn’t notice Jen Walters and her social worker Janine coming up to talk with them or asking her questions. No one really blamed her for not answering or paying attention. She was just staring off in the distance, and picking at her fingernails, not realizing that they had started to bleed.

    Suddenly, she feels someone place their hands over hers, and she looks up to see that Natasha has crouched down to her eye level, looking at her with a concerned expression.

    “Did you hear Jen, dorogoy?” She asks softly. Kate shakes her head no. Natasha gives her hands a comforting squeeze, “She asked if you are still comfortable about letting them use the interview you did with them last week and the record of your ER visits. You can still say no. And remember, you won’t have to answer any questions during or after the trial.”

    Kate looks up to see Jen, Clint, and Wanda all looking at her with soft, reassuring expressions.

    “Yeah, they can still use them.” Kate replies, quietly yet firmly.

    “Okay,” Jen says, sending Kate a soft smile, “The trial will be starting soon. They will let you into the room in a few minutes.”

    “Thank you, Jen,” Natasha says. Jen goes to go join the rest of her team, and Janine goes to talk with some coworkers. A few minutes later, someone comes up to tell them they can go into the courtroom.

    “Well,” Kate says, standing up and shaking off her nerves, “lets go get this over with.”

    Sitting in between Natasha and Clint, with Wanda on the other side of Natasha, Kate feels like she can get through anything.

    However, that feeling falters significantly when Eleanor walks into the room. Kate feels her whole body tense up. Eleanor’s eyes lock with hers and she feels her heart rate pick up significantly. Natasha immediately takes her hand in hers and starts to rub soothing circles on the top of her hand with her thumb and Clint puts his arm around the back of her chair.

    “If you need to get up or want to leave at any point, dorogoy, let me know and we will go, okay?” Natasha whispers to her. Kate nods back to her.

    The judge enters and the trial starts.

    If Kate is being honest, she is not 100% sure she knows exactly what is going on.

    All she knows is that Eleanor’s current defense strategy is that she was acting out of fear and protection for Kate. And so far, it seems to be working, much to Kate’s dismay. The jury is eating up every word she and her attorney say. Eleanor is currently on the witness stand and pouring her heart out on how scared she was for Kate. Kate just rolls her eyes. That woman has never once cared about my wellbeing. And isn’t it against the law to lie on the witness stand?

    The tides start to turn, however, when Jen Walters approaches the stand.

    “So, Mrs. Bishop,” Jen says as she walks closer to Eleanor, “you claim that you have acted the way you did out of concern and protection of your daughter, Katherine?”

    “Yes of course,” Eleanor replies sweetly, “she is the light of my life.”

    Some of the jury members even ‘aw’ at this. Kate just rolls her eyes.

    Give me a fucking break.

    “So, would you say that you try to protect Kate in all aspects of life? That you are a good mother?”

    At this Eleanor looks affronted, “What? Of course, I am a good mother! And as a good mother, I only want what every mother wants: her child to be safe and away from harm.”

    Kate huffs and rolls her eyes again. Natasha squeezes her hand again, a little tighter.

    “In that case I would like to introduce Exhibit H1 and H2 into evidence. A recorded interview with Katherine Bishop and a detailed copy of various emergency room visits of Katherine Bishop,” Jen says.

    Eleanor suddenly looks panicked, almost knowing what is about to come, as they start to play Kate’s interview for the whole court and pass out the copies of Kate's medical files to the jury and judge. When Kate sees herself onscreen, talking about various times Eleanor had sent her to the ER, she turns to see Eleanor giving her a terrifying glare.

    It feels like years before the video is over, but once it is, Kate feels the whole courtroom look over at her.

    Her heartrate automatically picks up.

    Eleanor lets out a demeaning, and almost villainous laugh.

    Kate feels the bile rising in her throat.

    “You stupid brat!” She yells to Kate across the courtroom, “You really think they are going to believe you? That anyone else will ever love you like I will? What a joke! If I get stuck in here, it will be all your fault you insipid little bitch!” Then two guards try to calm her down, and she fights against them continuing to laugh.

    In an instant, Kate is up and out of her seat, and sprinting towards the courtroom doors with Natasha, Wanda, and Clint on her heels.

    She runs all the way until she finds a bathroom, runs in the closest stall, and throws up. Natasha is there a second later, holding her hair, rubbing her back, and whispering soothing words to her. Once she is done, she wipes her mouth with some napkins and washes her hands. Wanda appears with a water bottle and Kate gratefully takes a few sips before sitting on a bench in the middle of the bathroom.

    “Are you ok, malen’kiy yastreb?” Natasha asks.

    And Kate doesn’t know if it was the fact that she just told an entire courtroom that her mother abused her, that she just threw up her guts, or simply because of how soft Natasha is talking to her; but tears immediately start to run down Kate’s face.

    The next thing she knows, she is in Natasha’s arms, and Wanda is rubbing soothing circles on her back; the both of them whispering reassuring words.

    After a little while, Kate’s tears slow down and she slowly pulls out of Natasha’s arms.

    Natasha reaches up and wipes the tears off of her cheeks.

    “Do you want to go back in? We don’t have to, we could go home, or we could go to a waiting room?” Natasha asks her, and Kate thinks for a moment.

    I sure as hell don’t want to go back in there, but I want to know what happens.

    “Could we go into the waiting room?” Kate asks, softly.

    “Of course, dorogoy.” Natasha replies, takes her hand and leads her out of the bathroom.

    When they exit the bathroom Kate looks up to Clint and Wanda.

    “Can you guys come with us too? To the waiting room?” Kate asks them, wanting them to stay her.

    “Of course, Katie-bug,” Wanda says, and Kate smiles at the nickname.

    “Sure thing Hawkeye, we are partners,” Clint replies, sending her a small grin, “wherever you go, I go, remember?”

    Kate sends them both grateful looks as they head to the waiting room.

    About an hour or so minutes later, Jen Walters and Social Worker Janine enter the room.

    They all stand up.

    “Is it over?” Clint asks the question on all their minds.

    “Yeah,” Jen starts, “it’s over. She was found guilty of both the murder and of parental neglect and child abuse. After Kate's interview and her files, the case was basically closed. The jury voted unanimously and she was sentenced to 45 years to life. As we talked about before, all of the press has signed non-disclosure agreements about the parental neglect and child abuse portion of her trial, so the public will not know about that and Kate’s information will be kept confidential.”

    Everyone in the room breathed a collective relieved sigh.

    Ok that is good, but what about me?

    Kate was just about to ask, but Natasha beat her to it.

    “So, what does this mean for Kate?” Natasha asks.

    This time Janine speaks up, “The judge found Eleanor to be unfit to be a mother, and was about to take her parental rights away, when Eleanor decided that she wanted to sign her parental rights to Kate away.”

    “Does this mean what I think it means?” Natasha asks hopefully as she looks at Jen and Janine.

    “It indeed does,” Jen replies with a smile, and Janine smiles too.

    Kate just looks at all of them confused. What are they talking about? Why is everyone smiling?

    “I actually had Janine draft all the paperwork up for you after that call we had a few weeks ago. So we are all good to go,” Jen adds.

    “What paperwork? What is going on?” Kate asks, nervously, looking from person to person in the room.

    Natasha looks at her with a soft smile, and takes her hand.

    “Do you guys mind if I talk to Kate alone for a minute? We will be out soon to get started on that paperwork.” Natasha says.

    “Of course, take your time.” Janine replies, and everyone files out of the room, leaving Natasha and Kate alone. Kate looks up at Natasha.

    “Tasha, what’s going on?” Kate asks, and Natasha has her sit down and she sits down next to her but turns so she can look at her. Natasha takes Kate’s hand.

    “A few weeks ago, when we got back from your visit with your mom, I called Jen to ask if there was a way to get permanent custody of you. She said that we would be able to revisit it once we found out what happened at your mother’s trial. But now that she has signed away her rights, it makes things easier.”

    “You—you mean you want me to stay with you?” Kate looks at Natasha with slight hope and some tears in her eyes.

    “Of course, malen’kiy yastreb!” Natasha says honestly, “After going through what I went through with the Red Room, I never thought I would be able to have a child and I have always mourned that fact. But these last few weeks has made me realize that I have just been waiting for you,” Natasha’s eyes start to tear up as well.

    “So what happens now?” Kate asks, trying to keep her tears at bay.

    “Well, if it is alright with you, I would really like to adopt you and get full custody; because I love you malen’kiy yastreb.” Natasha replies. When Kate doesn’t reply right away, she continues, “I mean Janine already has all the paperwork— oof” Kate practically throws herself into Natasha’s arms nodding, tears streaming down her cheeks.

    “I love you too.” Kate replies softly.

    After a few minutes of comfortable silence Kate leans back to look Natasha in the eye and asks, “Does this mean I am a Romanoff, now?”

    Natasha chuckles as her own tears finally break free, bringing Kate into a tighter hug.

    After what seems like an hour worth of paperwork alone, Wanda and Clint are witnesses to a very quick and small hearing with a judge in which Natasha is granted full custody of Kate, who is now officially Katherine Elizabeth Romanoff. It feels right.

    News must’ve made it back to the Compound before them because as they walk into their apartment Tony, Pepper, Morgan, Bruce, Vision, Steve, Bucky, and Sam are all waiting for them with Kate’s favorite pizza and a huge cake.

    Kate has never felt lighter in her whole life.

    Once everyone leaves, Natasha and Kate curl up on the couch and watch a movie. Kate falls asleep on Natasha’s shoulder within the first 20 minutes, but Natasha doesn’t get up to move her until the credits roll. She carries her to bed and Kate has the best sleep she has had in years.

Natasha drove as Clint sat in the passenger seat, Wanda and Kate sat in the back seat, and Lucky laid down in the trunk.     Sick Day Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes A week or so into March, Kate had fallen into a routine. Wake up, go to school, go to archery practice, come home, do homework, eat dinner, take a shower, watch TV with Natasha and Wanda (as Wanda had basically been living with them at this point whether or not Wanda and Natasha admitted it or not), then go to bed.

    And for the most part, it went without hitch most days, and Kate had finally felt herself settling back into normalcy.

    Until Friday morning.

    Kate woke up with a weird feeling in her head and a slightly scratchy throat. She just shrugged it off, blaming allergies and not sleeping well the night before. However, throughout the day, she started to feel worse and worse. Her head became progressively more and more stuffed up, and the scratch in her throat had turned painful.

    By the time she was getting ready for bed, she was pretty sure she had a cold. She didn’t want to tell Natasha for a few reasons. Mostly because she didn’t want to bother her, she was used to dealing with these things by herself, and she was really just going to avoid it and hopefully it would be gone by Monday.

    It is just a cold, what's the worst that could happen? I bet all I need is a goodnight’s sleep.

    Saturday morning, Natasha and Wanda were relaxing on the couch watching TV when Natasha looks down at the time on her phone and frowns.

    “What is wrong, detka?” Wanda asks her, seeing her worried face.

    “Nothing,” Natasha responds, “It’s just that it is almost 11 and Kate is still in bed. I know she is probably tired from this week, but she has never slept this late.”

    “Do you think she had a nightmare last night and we didn’t hear it?” Wanda suggests.

    “I don’t think so, she has been telling me lately when she has one. Did you notice how tired she looked last night? I wonder if she is feeling okay.”

    Now Wanda frowns, “Now that you mention it, she did sound a little stuffy last night, but I thought she was just tearing up at the episode of She-ra we were watching.”

    “I think I am going to go check on her,” Natasha says, getting up from the couch and walking towards Kate’s room, with Wanda following close behind. She knocks on Kate’s door, “Kate?”

    She gets no response so she knocks again, “Dorogoy? Are you awake?”

    After hearing a muffled whine behind the door from Lucky, and she decides to open the door. Pushing the door open, she sees that Kate is still in bed, with Lucky curled up next to her. Walking over, she notices that Kate is laying on her side, has her sweatshirt on, that she has every blanket covering her, and honestly a concerning number of tissues on the floor. Natasha sits on the side of the bed, and slowly starts to shake Kate’s shoulder to wake her up, as Wanda starts to clean up the scattered tissues.

    “Kate? It’s time to wake up malen’kiy yastreb.” At this, Kate just whines and rolls onto her back. Natasha immediately notices that her nose is red and her cheeks are flushed. “Come on, dorogoy. Let’s wake up.”

    Kate’s eyes open, glassy and unfocused, “I promise I will finish the chores and the laundry later, I am too tired right now. Just don’t get the belt,” Kate slurs.

    Natasha’s worry is immediately increased. She touches Kate’s cheek, and is surprised to feel heat coming off in waves; she feels a pit form in her stomach.

    She looks over to Wanda, “She is burning up,” then she looks back down at Kate, “No, no, malen’kiy yastreb. It’s me. It’s Tasha,” she says.

    She watches as Kate starts to look around slowly and begins to recognize where she is. When Kate's hazy gaze lands on her, Natasha sends her a comforting smile as she continues to soothingly caress her cheek.

    “Oh, hey Tasha,” Kate replies with a dazed smile, leaning into Natasha's touch, “I have been looking for you.”

    “You have?” Natasha says with a small grin, slightly confused.

    “It’s okay now because you are here,” Kate responds, still slightly slurring, but sounding very relieved. Something about the way Kate says that pulls at Natasha’s heartstrings.

    “I’m glad. But I think you have a fever malen’kiy yastreb; we need to get these layers off of you and probably get you some medicine.”

    Shit, I don’t think we have any Tylenol. And I know for sure that I don’t have any other cold/flu supplies. How did I not know she was this sick?

    As Natasha is thinking, Kate starts to fall back asleep, “No, no dorogoy, you need to stay awake for a few minutes for me. Wanda? Can you grab the thermometer? It should be in my bathroom medicine cabinet.”

    “Of course,” Wanda says, and within a minute, returns with the thermometer. Natasha takes it and coaxes it into a very delirious Kate’s mouth. In another minute, it beeps. 103.1. Fuck. Natasha turns to Wanda with very wide eyes, and watches as Wanda’s eyes widen when she sees the number blinking on the device.

    What do we do? What do we do? Shit.

    “Um, okay. Wanda, can you call Helen to see if she can come and check Kate?” Natasha asks her, “I am going to try and get her into some cooler clothes. I don’t even have any medicine for her. Why didn’t I plan ahead for this? I mean she’s a kid, she was bound to get sick at some point. I should've noticed sooner, I am a goddamn spy. Now she is really sick. I am such a bad—”

    “Detka,” Wanda puts her hands on Natasha’s shoulders to stop her from her spiral, “like you said, she is a kid. Kid’s get sick. And they also bounce back quickly. You are not a bad parent. If anything, the fact that you care this much and are this worried proves you are an amazing parent,” she smiles softly when Natasha takes a deep breath. “I am going to go call Cho, you try to cool Kate down.”

    “Okay,” Natasha sends her a grateful smile, “thank you.”

    Wanda just sends her a soft smile and kisses her forehead, then she leaves to go call Dr. Cho. Lucky, knowing his favorite human is not feeling good, is snuggled up to Kate’s side; but with a little prodding from Natasha, he jumps off the bed. Natasha then starts to remove the blankets covering Kate. Damn Kate. I didn’t even know we had this many blankets. When she pulls the last one off, Kate whines and tries to curl up into a tight ball.

    “I know, milaya; but we need to get you cooler before you get too sick,” Natasha soothes. When she takes off Kate’s sweatshirt, Kate’s whines start to turn into tears, breaking Natasha’s heart.

    “No Tasha! It’s too cold! It hurts,” Kate cries with tears running down her face. Natasha quickly changes Kate into a cooler t-shirt and gathers her up in her arms.

    “Shh, I know malyshka, I know.” Natasha starts to rub circles on Kate’s back, “shhh, it’s okay.”

    Kate slowly starts to calm and relaxes further into Natasha’s arms, tucking her head under Natasha’s chin, only whimpering every once in a while.

    Then Wanda walks back in, “She said she will be up in about a half hour. With the small list of symptoms I gave her, she is pretty positive it’s the flu.” She sits on the side of the bed next to where Natasha is holding Kate.

    They sit in semi-silence for a little while, until Kate suddenly pulls away from Natasha and rushes into the bathroom. She barely opens the toilet lid in time to throw up. Natasha pulls her hair away from her face, rubs her back, and whispers some soothing words. Wanda gets her a face cloth and some water.

    After taking a sip of water and wiping her face, Natasha asks her, “Are you okay, moy malen’kiy yastreb? Do you feel like you need to throw up more?”

    Kate’s half-lidded, glazed, tearful eyes meet Natasha’s and she hesitates for a second before shaking her head no.

    “Do you want to go cuddle in bed while we wait for Dr. Cho?”

    Kate answers quicker this time with a nod.

    They head back into Kate’s room and they go back to where they were sitting before. Wanda reaches over and turns on the TV to help distract Kate from her discomfort.

    While they are waiting for Dr. Cho, Natasha starts to notice something dripping on her neck, near where Kate has tucked her head. She looks down to see tears running down Kate’s cheek.

    “Oh dorogoy, what’s wrong?” She asks softly, reaching to wipe a tear off her cheek.

    “I just don’t feel good Mama.”

    Natasha’s whole world pauses.

    Mama.

    She knows that Kate is delirious from a 103-degree fever, and probably doesn’t even know what she is saying, but the word still rocks her. Her heart feels like it’s about to explode. That warm feeling that she gets from time-to-time becomes almost too intense to bear. She looks up to see Wanda already looking at her with a soft, loving expression. Then she looks back down to Kate finding her half asleep.

    She softly kisses Kate's temple and wipes away some more tears off Kate's cheek.

    “I know, baby, I know.”

    I would go to the ends of the Earth for this kid.

    Kate falls back asleep in Natasha's arms before Dr. Cho comes, Natasha and Wanda let her sleep. It is clear that she is uncomfortable. She keeps shifting in Natasha's embrace and whimpering every once and a while. When Dr. Cho comes, she concludes that Kate does, indeed, have the flu. Natasha wakes Kate up briefly to give her the medicine Dr. Cho brings, and within an hour, they can already tell that her fever has gone down as Kate has started to sleep more peacefully.

    Once Kate’s fever has gone down more, Wanda volunteers to go out and get some over the counter medicine, some electrolyte rich drinks, supplies for chicken noodle soup, and ginger ale. While she is out, Natasha stays with Kate, sitting next to her watching TV, just in case she needs anything.

    Kate starts to stir, and looks around, “Tasha?”

    Natasha immediately turns her attention to Kate, noting that her eyes no longer look as glossy and unfocused as they were when she first woke up, “Hey malen’kiy yastreb. How are you feeling?”

    “Like I have been hit by a truck. What time is it? What happened?” Her voice is a little rough.

    “It’s almost 5:00, you have been asleep most of the day. You woke up earlier with a pretty high fever, we had to call Dr. Cho to come check on you and bring you some medicine. You have the flu. Can I take your temperature while you are awake?"

    Kate nods. Natasha hands her the thermometer to put under her tongue, and in a minute it beeps. 100.7 degrees. Oh thank god. Natasha feels a weight lift off her shoulders, "Do you remember anything?”

    “I kinda remember having to throw up? And I remember you and Wanda being there; everything else is a blur,” Kate sends her a soft smile, “but, thank you, for, well, everything.” “You never need to thank me for these things, dorogoy,” Natasha says sincerely, “Why didn’t you tell us sooner that you didn’t feel good?”

    “I didn’t want to bother you,” Kate shrugs, “And I am kinda used to dealing with these things by myself. Eleanor never helped me or wanted me near her when I was sick.”

    Natasha shifts closer on the bed, and Kate takes this opportunity to cuddle back up in her arms, tucking her head back under Natasha’s chin. Natasha gives her a tight hug.

    “Next time you don’t feel good, or god forbid get an injury, I don’t care where I am or what I am doing; you come tell me okay? And if for some reason you can’t find or reach me, you tell Wanda, okay?”

    Kate nods, “Okay.”

    Then Wanda walks in, “Hey Nat, I am back, how’s Kate? Oh! You are awake!” Wanda smiles brightly, “you really had us worried there for a little while. How are you feeling?”

    “Better than I was this morning.”

    “I am glad. Now, scooch over you too! While we are waiting for the chicken soup I started to finish, we are going to watch the ultimate sick day movie: The Princess Bride!” Wanda says.

    A few hours later, after the movie and amazing chicken soup, Kate finds herself barely able to stay awake. Natasha and Wanda both notice this and suggest they get ready for bed. Once Kate is changed and comfortable in bed, and despite looking like she is going to fall asleep sitting up, she keeps herself awake. Wanda and Natasha can feel her hesitation.

    “Why don’t you lay down and get comfy, Katie-bug?” Wanda suggests. Kate shrugs.

    “Um, Tasha?” Kate asks nervously, looking at Natasha, “would you want to, I mean you most certainly don’t have to and I totally get it, but would you want to stay with me?”

    Natasha and Wanda instantly send her warm smiles.

    “Of course, malen’kiy yastreb,” Natasha says as she gets in, and Kate immediately goes back to how she was cuddling before, resting her head on Natasha’s chest.

    Wanda reaches over and squeezes Kate’s shoulder, “Get some good rest, bug.”

    “Goodnight, Wanda,” Kate replies, as her eyes slip closed.

    Wanda then gives Natasha a quick kiss and says, “Goodnight, detka. Let me know if you guys need anything.”

    “Goodnight, moya lyubov,” Natasha smiles to Wanda, “Could you just grab the thermometer from Kate's bathroom and put it on the bedside table before you leave in case we need it in the middle of the night?”

    “As you wish,” Wanda winks and does exactly that before shutting off the light and heading down the hall to Natasha’s room.

    Natasha slides down on the bed, and gets herself more comfortable. She feels Kate take a deep breath, and melt further into her embrace, making that warm feeling in Natasha's heart return.

    “Love you, Tasha,” Kate says sleepily, “goodnight.”

    “Love you too, malyshka,” Natasha replies as she places a kiss on Kate’s hairline.

    “Okay then,” Natasha says with a smirk, “I guess I will just go back to Russia.”

    Kate then looks up and turns around to see Natasha in the living room. Her whole face brightens, and she squeals.

    “Tasha!!” She runs up to Natasha, who had stood up to give Kate a hug, and launches herself into Natasha’s arms, “You are home!”

After a few minutes, Kate pulls back and Natasha presses a kiss to Kate’s hairline while wiping away the few tears. It is then when Kate notices the other woman in the room.

    “There is someone I want you to meet,” Natasha says to Kate, “This is Yelena. She is my sister.”

    Kate smiles at Yelena, and holds her hand out for Yelena to shake, which she does.

    “Yelena, this is Kate,” Natasha continues, “my daughter.”

    Daughter.

    “Yup,” Natasha says proudly, putting her arm around Kate who leans into her side, “I adopted her a few months ago. It was the best decision I have ever made in my life.”

    “She wouldn’t even let me put her down,” Natasha continues to chuckle, “good thing I already have my pajamas on and don’t need to use the bathroom.”

    “I really missed you,” Wanda says softly.

    Natasha cranes her neck to give Wanda a chaste kiss.

    “I missed you too, moya lyubov,” Natasha replies, then looks down at Kate, “this one too.” “Goodnight, detka,” Wanda says, “I love you.”

    “I love you too, Wanda.”

    The small family, finally reunited, fell into a deep, restful sleep.
