    “Nat, calm down,” Clint tries to defend, “he was just trying to help.”

    “Don’t even get me started,” Natasha turns to Clint, and is about to continue when she feels a hand on her shoulder. She turns around to see Laura with a soft smile.

    “Nat, I got this,” Laura says calmly, “why don’t you and Wanda help Kate get washed up and in dry clothes.”

    Natasha sighs.

    “I stand by my previous statement of ‘you should come with a warning label’,” Natasha says before taking Kate’s hand and leading her up the stairs to her and Wanda’s room.

    Once in the room, Wanda helps Kate get off her hoodie that seems to almost be stuck to her long sleeve while Natasha grabs her some dry clothes and starts the shower for her.

    “Are you okay bug?” Wanda asks, noticing Kate starting to shake a bit.

    “Y-yeah, I am j-just a little c-cold,” Kate replies, shivering.

    Wanda reaches out and feels Kate’s cheek, “Your skin is like ice! Let’s get you warmed up.” “Okay baby,” Natasha says walking out of the bathroom, “the shower is all ready. It is not super warm right now, but that is because you are so cold. It needs to be warmed up slowly so your body doesn’t go into shock.”

    “Okay Mama,” Kate nods.

    “We will be here when you get out,” Wanda smiles.

    Kate returns the smile and heads into to bathroom.

    Kate isn’t sure if it is the long shower or just the whole ordeal, but as soon as she pulls on the comfy dry clothes her mama got for her and finishes brushing her hair, exhaustion hits her like a truck.

    When she walks out of the bathroom, she finds her parents sitting on the bed, peacefully watching TV. She wastes no time in crawling into the bed and wiggling right in between them.

    “Hey malen’kiy yastreb,” Natasha smiles down at Kate.

    “Hi Mama,” Kate smiles back, tiredly.

    “All warmed up, milaya?” Wanda asks.

    “Mhm,” Kate hums, and leans onto Natasha who immediately pulls her to rest in her arms.

    “Good. That was quite an adventure, huh?” Natasha says softly.

    Kate just nods, as her eyes start to droop and she melts deeper into Natasha’s embrace and she starts to fall asleep, “love you guys.”

    “We love you too, bug,” Wanda bends down to kiss Kate on the temple.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Before they left, Kate and Lila, after a long discussion that may or may not have included a PowerPoint, had managed to convince their parents to let Lila come stay a few nights at the Compound. In the end, the parents agree because Clint needs to go to the Compound in a few days to pick up some new arrowheads from Tony.

    “Yay!!” Lila cheers as she buckles herself into her seat on the Quinjet, “I am so excited!” “Me too!” Kate smiles as she sits next to her, “I can’t wait to show you all the cool spots I found to hang out in and my room!”

    “Cool! I am pretty sure your room is the one I used to stay in when I used to go visit Auntie Nat.”

    “Oh,” Kate pauses for a moment, “cool!”

    “I am excited to see what you have done with it!” Lila smiles.

    Kate sends her a small smile back.

    Something about that comment didn’t sit right with Kate. She couldn’t put a finger on the emotion that it evoked, but it created a small feeling in the pit of her stomach that she didn’t like.

    So, she changed topics.

    “Maybe even America can come over one day,” Kate says with a smirk, slightly louder towards the end so she can subtly ask her parents if the dark haired multiversal traveler could visit.

    “Yeah,” Lila smirks along, seeing what Kate was doing, and eggs her on, “I would LOVE to meet her.”

    Natasha chuckles from the driver’s seat, “We will see, malyshka.”

    After taking a quick pitstop to drop Melina and Alexei off at their jet, with enough fuel to refuel their jet for their trip back to Russia, the rest of the crew lands back at the Compound around 4:00 p.m.

    Kate wastes no time, pulling Lila along towards the apartment and showing her around. She shows Lila her favorite reading nook in the library, her archery target set up down at the range, her spot under the big white oak tree, and she is just about to open the door to the roof access when FRIDAY’s voice startles both of them.

    “Kate and Miss Barton, Agents Romanoff and Maximoff are requesting your presence for dinner,” the AI announces.

    Kate groans and turns around, “Darn! We will have to check out the roof later or tomorrow then.”

    “No worries!” Lila says as they start to walk back to the apartment, “I have actually already been on the roof. Auntie Nat used to take me up there all the time when we first got back from the Blip.”

    Again, the way Lila says that makes that weird feeling Kate had earlier come back. And yet again, it confuses Kate.

    What the heck is that? Maybe I just didn’t get enough sleep last night, or its just over excitement at the fact Lila is here in the first place??

    “Oh… cool!” Kate replies, “We could hang out up there tomorrow if the weather is nice.”

    “That sounds fun!”

    “Yeah, maybe then you can finally update me on what is going on with Alex from geometry,” Kate chuckles.

    Lila laughs and half-heartedly pushes Kate. They continue to chat and laugh as they enter the apartment. Natasha and Wanda both smile.

    “What’s so funny?” Wanda smirks.

    “Oh nothing,” Kate chuckles.

    “You had to be there,” Lila adds, as they both walk over to the kitchen.

    “Oh goodness,” Natasha playfully smiles as she turns off the burner that she had been cooking on, “something tells me we are gonna have our hands full with these girls the next few days, moya lyubov.”

    “I don’t know if we will be able to handle it detka,” Wanda adds amusingly, then address the girls, “why don’t you two go wash up, dinner is ready.”

    “Okay Mom!” Kate says then her and Lila go to wash their hands in the hall bathroom.

    When they return; Wanda, Natasha, and Yelena are all sitting at the table in their respective seats: Natasha at the head, Wanda to her left, and Yelena in her seat next to the one Kate normally sits at on the right of Natasha. Kate goes to make her way over to her seat, when Lila goes and sits right at that spot instead.

    Kate hesitates, and the rest of the table notices.

    “Oh! I am sorry!” Lila says, “Is this your normal spot?”

    “It’s okay!” Kate tries to reassure, both Lila and herself, and goes to sit next to Wanda, “I can sit next to Mom tonight.”

    “Are you sure?” Lila asks, “I can move?”

    “No, its totally fine,” Kate promises, even though deep down she doesn’t really believe herself, “plus I think Mom was starting to get jealous that I wasn’t sitting next to her,” she finishes with a half-hearted chuckle, and sends Lila a smile.

    Lila smiles back and they start to eat dinner.

    But that feeling in Kate’s stomach comes back with a vengeance, and with it comes a sadness that Kate can’t place. If Wanda and Natasha notice that she doesn’t eat as much as she normally would, neither comment on it.

    After dinner, they watch a movie before heading to bed. Kate and Lila are giggling and getting ready for bed in Kate’s room when Natasha sticks her head in.

    “Don’t stay up too late, girls,” Natasha reminds them with a smirk, before closing the door. “We won’t Mama!” Kate calls back, chuckling.

    As they get into bed, they chat for a little while, but ultimately, they end up falling asleep about a half hour after they turned off the lights.

    Well, Lila fell asleep.

    Kate, on the other hand, was quite restless. She was rather confused as to why. The feeling in her gut had lessened significantly since dinner, but it still lingered. As well as the sadness. Normally, in moments like this, Kate would get up and go snuggle with Natasha and Wanda, as they would instantly make her feel calmer and relaxed. But this was the first sleepover she has ever had at her own home, and she wanted to be a good host. So, she reached over and grabbed her phone, turned the brightness all the way down, and started to read her e-book of The Hunger Games.

    The first full day Lila was there, her and Kate spent most of the day all by themselves. They hung around the Compound, giggling and chatting. And by the time dinner came around, Kate didn’t even notice how little sleep she got the night before.

    There was still little comments Lila made throughout the day that made that weird feeling in Kate’s stomach come back, but for the most part she brushed it off.

    They didn’t seem to affect her til she was back in bed for the night.

    She was hoping since she hadn’t slept well the night before that she would definitely sleep well tonight. But with all these unstable thoughts and feelings, Kate still couldn’t fall asleep. So, she repeated her actions from the previous night. She grabbed her phone and pulled up her e-book, hoping she would fall asleep sooner than she did last night.

    The next morning, Kate woke up with a headache and with Lila no where in sight. Sitting up, she looks at her alarm clock to see that it is 9:45 a.m., meaning she only got about three and a half hours of sleep. She sighs, slowly getting out of bed, stopping to throw her hoodie over her head before heading out into the kitchen.

    Walking out of her room, she hears laughter emanating from the kitchen. When she reaches the source of the noise, she finds Lila and Natasha standing at the kitchen island baking muffins; both of them covered in flour.

    Natasha laughs then wipes Lila’s cheek with a rag, “You are covered, my sweet girl.”

    The unpleasant feeling in Kate’s stomach returns.

    And it hits her.

    She is jealous.

    But I kind of don’t really have any right to be… Mama has been Lila’s Aunt longer… than she has been my mama.

    A sadness starts to loom in the back of Kate’s mind.

    Before she can dwell on her thoughts further, Natasha notices Kate’s presence.

    “Hey there, malen’kiy yastreb! You sure slept late today,” Natasha says.

    “Yeah,” Kate sighs as she sits at the kitchen island, “so what are you guys making?”

    “Muffins!” Lila cheers, “They are chocolate chip!”

    “Awesome!” Kate smiles tiredly, “Those are my favorite!”

    “I know!” Lila says, “Mine too!”

    Kate smiles at her cousin. Despite the lingering feelings, she is still happy she is here. Soon, Wanda and Yelena join them and they have breakfast. Once they are done eating, they move into the living room to watch some TV before getting a start on their day. Lila talks about wanting to paint her fingernails, and Wanda jumps at the idea.

    “I would love too!” Wanda smiles, “Kate doesn’t really like to paint her nails all that often, and neither does Natasha for that matter, so I will gladly paint my nails with you!”

    Kate’s heart drops at that.

    Does Mom want to do nails more often? Why doesn’t she tell me? I feel bad.

    Natasha must’ve either noticed the forlorn look on Kate’s face or is still slightly concerned at the fact that Kate slept later than normal because she comes to sit next to Kate. Kate instantly slumps onto Natasha’s side, and Natasha’s arm comes around to hold her. Kate is surprised to feel her throat tighten and tears start to prick in her eyes. Taking a deep breath, she wills them away.

    “Are you okay, malen’kiy yastreb?” Natasha asks softly, so that only Kate can hear.

    Kate nods, “Just a little tired,” she replies softly.

    “Did you have a bad dream last night?” Natasha asks.

    Kate shakes her head no, “Just couldn’t sleep.”

    “Why didn’t you come snuggle with me and Mom?”

    Kate just shrugs.

    Natasha frowns. Kate is usually so much more open with her. She could understand Kate being embarrassed if this was a sleepover with Franny or Greer, but she doesn’t think this is the case here.

    Before she can ask Kate another question, she looks down to see her deeply asleep. She smiles sadly, and brushes a piece of hair out of Kate’s face.

    Kate wakes up a few hours later to a mostly empty and quiet apartment. Next to her on the couch, Wanda is peacefully reading whatever novel she is in the middle of with Liho in her lap. Kate sits up and rubs her eyes, noting to herself that despite getting some rest, her headache did not go away. In fact, it got slightly worse.

    Wanda smiles softly at Kate, “Hi there, bug. Did you have a good nap?”

    Kate shrugs, and moves to snuggle up with Wanda. Liho purring at Kate’s closeness.

    “What time is it?” Kate asks.

    “A little after 12:30,” Wanda supplies, “If you are hungry, I can make you some lunch if you want.”

    Kate shakes her head no, “I am okay,” then she looks around the rest of the apartment, “where is everyone?”

    “Mama and Yelena took Lila out to the mall,” Wanda says.

    “Oh.”

    “They were going to see if you wanted to come, but you looked really tired.”

    Kate nods softly.

    A burst of sadness and a slight bit of anger runs through her. She shifts away from Wanda and pushes herself up from the couch. Wanda looks up at Kate in confusion.

    Stretching slightly and sending Wanda a waxy smile, Kate says, “I think I am gonna run down to the gym.”

    “Okay, bug,” Wanda replies, still a little concerned, “are you okay?”

    “Yeah, I’m fine,” Kate brushes her off, “just wanna get moving after lounging all day.”

    “Okay,” Wanda says, still unconvinced, “I will let you know when the others get back.”

    Kate nods, then heads to her room to get changed then goes right down to the gym.

    Around dinner time; Natasha, Lila, and Yelena finally arrive back from the mall. Lila goes to put her things in Kate’s room when Natasha asks where Kate is. Wanda informs her that Kate has been down at the gym all day and Natasha is immediately concerned, as it is something they both do when they are not feeling the best emotionally. Before they can continue their conversation further, Lila comes into the kitchen and asks what they are having for dinner. Yelena is sent to go retrieve Kate from the gym once dinner is ready. Walking in, Yelena can see that Kate is at the punching bag, instead of at the range, and she is not fooling around. As Sonja would say, ‘she is teaching that bag a lesson.’

    Yelena walks over to her and lets out a low whistle and chuckles, “Damn, Kate, what did that bag ever do to you?”

    Kate tears her eyes away from what she is doing and sends a stony look over to Yelena.

    Usually in the gym, Kate can work out her feelings and return to her normal feeling self. This time, however, her anger only built. Her thoughts compounding into one another, turning against themselves so rapidly Kate could hardly keep up.

    “Woah, hey,” Yelena puts her hands up in defense, “I just came to bring you up for dinner.” “Well, I am not hungry,” Kate states simply before starting to hit the bag again.

    Yelena frowns.

    “Hey,” Yelena says softly, putting her hand on Kate’s shoulder and spinning her around to face her, “Are you okay, malen’kaya ptichka?”

    “WHY does everyone keep asking me that?” Kate snaps exasperatedly.

    “Kate—” Yelena starts concernedly.

    Kate shuts her eyes and sighs, “I am sorry. I am fine.”

    She opens them to find Yelena looking at her skeptically.

    “I promise,” Kate plasters a fake smile on her face, “come on, lets get back to the apartment before they send a whole search party.”

    They head back up to the apartment, and Kate runs into her room to get changed really fast and take off the wraps from when she was using the punching bag. As she takes them off, she realizes she must’ve been punching too hard and for too long because her knuckles are red, scratchy, and starting to be covered in angry bruises.

    Shit. Mama and Mom are definitely gonna notice.

    Sighing, she makes her way back out to the kitchen, only to find Lila in her seat again.

    Why didn’t anyone say anything? She got to spend the entire day with Mama. I guess she has more of a right to do that than I do.

    Kate goes and sits at the seat that she occupied the other night. She makes sure to put her hands in her hoodie pocket so that Natasha and Wanda don’t see the forming bruises.

    Everyone is talking about something that Kate can’t focus on. Natasha laughs at something Lila says, Wanda shaking her head at the two of them.

    Mama looks so happy.

    I can’t remember the last time I made her laugh like that.

    Am I not a good daughter?

    Kate looks up to see that the conversation has stopped and they are all looking at her.

    “What?” Kate says, defensively.

    “I just asked what you wanted?” Natasha supplies with a worried look, “We have chicken, mashed potatoes, corn—”

    “I am not hungry,” Kate says softly.

    “You gotta eat something, malyshka,” Natasha tries, “Mom said you didn’t eat lunch. And you know the rule: at least two full meals a day.”

    They had come up with that rule when Kate first moved in and it was apparent that she was not getting enough nutrients. They have kept it as Kate often simply forgets to eat sometimes; Natasha wouldn’t be surprised if Kate had ADHD.

    “Here, Kate, why don’t you have a little chicken and some mashed potatoes to start,” Wanda suggests, grabbing the chicken and making a move to put some on her plate.

    “I said: I am not hungry,” Kate repeats rigidly, in a annoyed tone crossing her arms.

    “And I said you have to eat something,” Natasha replies firmly, and nods to Wanda to put some food on Kate’s plate.

    Before she can manage, Kate pushes the plate away.

    “Kate!” Wanda says surprised. Natasha’s gaze narrows at her daughter.

    “It would just be a waste anyway, I am not going to eat it,” Kate grumbles, crossing her arms once again.

    “I know you aren’t feeling the best today,” Natasha adds, with the same firmness and a bit of discontentment at her daughter’s actions, “but I would like it you at least tried to eat something.”

    “No thanks, I am all set,” Kate answers back firmly.

    “Katherine,” Natasha’s voice takes on a more dangerous edge.

    “Natasha,” Kate replies in almost the same tone. She knows she is playing with fire, but at this point she doesn’t care. She expressed multiple times that she did not want to eat and her mother won’t listen to her.

    Her mother that didn’t even bother to wake her up to see if she wanted to spend time with her aunt and her cousin at the mall.

    Natasha’s gaze becomes annoyed with a slight bit of hurt in it.

    “That is NOT my name,” Natasha says unwaveringly.

    Kate looks around the table to see everyone looking back at her with mixed expressions of shock, and she suddenly feels way too overwhelmed. She can feel her heart rate pick up. She puts her hands on the table and pushes herself up to stand.

    “Where are you going?” Natasha asks.

    Kate doesn’t answer, she just turns to go back to her room, but is stopped by Wanda gently grabbing her wrist.

    When she looks back, she notices Wanda looking at the marks on her knuckles. Wanda looks up at her very concerned.

    “What happened, milaya?” she asks softly.

    Kate just shakes her head no, and tries to softly tug her hand away from Wanda. This unsettles Wanda further and she holds on slightly tighter, not letting Kate escape.

    Kate feels tears prick her eyes once again and lets out a soft, “Please Mom.”

    Wanda’s heart breaks. She nods and lets Kate’s wrist go. As soon as her arm is free, Kate all but sprints to her room, shutting the door.

    Once she is inside, she collapses onto the bed, sobbing.

    An hour or so later, Kate hears a knocking at her door.

    “Baby?” she can hear Natasha call through the door, “It’s Mama. Can I come in?”

    Kate does not respond, only wraps herself further into her blankets. She hears her door click open and immediately evens her breathing and shuts her eyes to feign sleep. She feels her mother come over and sit on the side of the bed.

    Natasha looks down to see that Kate is either very lightly asleep or is pretending to sleep. Deciding not to push her into opening up, she just reaches down and softly brushes some hair out of her eyes.

    Her heart twists painfully when she notices the dried tear marks and the blotchiness on her face.

    Kate expects her to just leave now that she knows she is ‘sleeping,’ but Natasha just stays. She continues to softly stroke Kate’s cheek. Between Natasha’s presence and her gentle touch, Kate feels her racing thoughts start to slow and herself start to actually fall asleep. She lets out a soft, almost pained sigh, as she relaxes further into the bed.

    Natasha stays until she is sure Kate is actually asleep. When she is, Natasha leans down and kisses her temple, “I love you, baby. Get some rest.”

    A few hours pass and Kate is started awake by the bed dipping next to her.

    “Sorry!” Lila whispers, “I was trying to not wake you up.”

    “It’s okay,” Kate whispers back, sincerely, turning on her side to face Lila.

    “Are you okay, Katie?” Lila asks, concern coloring her gaze, “Auntie Nat said you weren’t feeling well. You can tell me anything.”

    Kate smiles sadly.

    She knows that none of this is Lila’s fault.

    “I am okay,” she says softly, then she lies to spare Lila’s feelings, “I got my period today.” She doesn’t deserve to feel bad because of me and my feelings.

    Understanding washes over Lila’s face, and she leans over to give Kate a hug, “Oh. I am sorry Katie.”

    “It’s all good,” Kate chuckles softly, “my period is usually really all over the place, hence the heightened emotions.”

    Lila chuckles back, “I totally get it. You should have seen my mom when she got her first period after having Nate.”

    The girls both start to laugh.

    In the morning, Clint comes early to pick up Lila. After a quick goodbye, Kate runs back into her room. Wanda and Natasha send each other a somber look and decide they will go talk to her in a little while. Natasha had told Wanda about the state she had found their daughter in the night before and they both had an idea as to what was bothering the young archer.

    A little while later, they both go over to Kate’s door and knock. After hearing a quiet, “come in,” they push open the door to find Kate sitting on her bed looking out the window with Liho in her lap.

    “Hey milaya,” Wanda says warmly as they both walk in and sit on opposite sides of Kate.

    Kate sends them both a brief small smile before looking down at the black little kitten sitting in her lap.

    “I’m sorry,” Kate whispers so softly, Natasha and Wanda have to strain to hear it.

    “What are you sorry about, baby?” Natasha asks.

    “I didn’t mean to act like that yesterday I just, ugh,” Kate sighs, “I dunno. My thoughts have been all over the place lately.”

    “Its okay bug,” Wanda says.

    Kate sighs.

    “We think we know why,” Natasha starts gently. Kate looks up at her in confusion, “Baby we love Lila. She is our niece and quite possibly the reason I realized I wanted to have kids of my own.”

    Kate frowns and looks down at her hands. Natasha softly takes her chin in her hand and lifts her head so she can meet her eyeline.

    “But she isn’t you,” Natasha smiles sadly, “You are my kid. You are my daughter.”

    Tears, that Kate didn’t realize were starting to build in her eyes start to run down her cheeks. “But you have known her longer than me,” Kate sniffles, “she’s your sweet girl.”

    Natasha’s heart breaks. She takes the cat out of Kate’s lap and pulls Kate into her arms, holding her tight.

    “She may be my sweet girl,” Natasha kisses Kate’s temple, “but you are moy malen’kiy yastreb. Moya malyshka.”

    Kate pushes her head into Natasha neck, “I love you, Mama.”

    “I love you too, baby.”

    Once Kate’s tears slow, she pulls back and Natasha wipes the tears off her face.

    “I’m sorry, I didn’t mean for my silly feelings to mess up everything,” Kate frowns.

    “You didn’t mess up anything bug,” Wanda reassures, “And your feelings aren’t silly.”

    “To be honest,” Natasha says with a slight smirk, “I would’ve been a little concerned if you didn’t act just a little bit jealous,” she finishes with a chuckle.

    Kate playfully hits her on the arm, starting to chuckle too.

    “What do you say we have a Mom, Mama, Katie-bug day?” Wanda suggests.

    Kate smiles and snuggles back into Natasha.

    “That sounds perfect.”

    Important Questions Chapter Notes This one is a bit shorter but it is no less important -- if not more! :)

    See the end of the chapter for more notes The next day, Wanda was called on a mission, much to the disappointment of Natasha and Kate. But Kate quickly shakes it off, as best as she can, and comes up with an amazing idea on how to spend the time her mom is away.

    “Hey mama!” Kate says as she sits down next to Natasha on the couch.

    “Hey malen’kiy yastreb! What’s up?” Natasha smiles.

    “I know it sucks that Mom is gone, but I thought we could use this time to go out to the mall or something to get her Christmas gifts,” Kate suggests.

    “That is a great idea, malyshka! Why don’t you go grab your shoes and we can go.”

    After wandering around the mall for a while, Kate had pretty much finished all of her Christmas shopping for everyone she needed to buy for.

    Well, for everyone except Natasha of course.

    Natasha also seemed to get a good chunk of her shopping done, mostly for the rest of the team and the Barton kids.

    “Do you wanna grab some lunch while we are here? Or do you wanna go home and have a picnic on the roof?” Natasha asks.

    “Oooo!” Kate hums, “On one hand, I LOVE mall food court food. On the other hand, it is just starting to snow, so it would look really cool sitting under the dome on the roof. BUT we don’t have this food at home. Although, I don’t really feel like sitting here in the mall to eat.” Natasha chuckles at her daughter’s antics.

    “Ugh! I dunno,” Kate groans, “Mama, you just pick.”

    “Easy, dorogoy,” Natasha continues to chuckle, “Why don’t we just order some food here then take it back home?”

    Kate sighs, “You are the smartest person I have ever met.”

    Natasha puts her arm around Kate’s shoulders and kisses her temple.

    They both order of the best, in Kate’s words, ‘crappy but amazing’ mall food and head home. They take it up to the glass domed roof. They sit under the dome and watch the snow hit the glass as they enjoy their lunch, enjoying the comfortable peace around them.

    Once they are done eating, Natasha gets up and sits behind Kate, pulling her back into her arms. Kate immediately melts into the embrace. Kate smiles and closes her eyes.

    “Are you gonna fall asleep on me, malen’kiy yastreb?” Natasha whispers, feeling the need to talk softly as to not break the peace around them.

    “No,” Kate answers back just as softly, eyes still closed, “just enjoying this.”

    Natasha smiles, and tightens her arms around Kate.

    After a few moments, “Actually, dorogoy,” Natasha says, “Can I ask you something?”

    Kate opens her eyes and leans back slightly to look Natasha in the eye, slightly concerned, “What’s up?”

    Natasha sends her an easy smile to Kate to hopefully calm her nerves, “So I have been thinking… of a few things actually. And they kinda coincide with each other.”

    “Okay,” Kate says slowly, skeptically “Don’t worry,” Natasha chuckles, “It’s just about good things! … Well, I hope good things…”

    Kate chuckles, “What did you want to ask me?”

    “Oh! Right,” Natasha smiles, “So, you know me and Wanda have been together for a long time. And obviously, you know she means a lot to me, and I know she means a lot to you as well.”

    Kate feels a grin starting to dawn on her face, because she knows exactly where this is going. “I was wondering, how you would feel,” Natasha hesitates, almost as if she is a little nervous, “if I asked Wanda to marry me?”

    Kate pauses for a second before she squeals and jumps back into her mother’s arms.

    Natasha is slightly shocked by the reaction before laughing, “Does this mean you are okay with that?”

    “Okay?!?” Kate cries, “I am way beyond okay! I have been researching engagement rings and wedding venues since April!!”

    Natasha hugs Kate tighter as they both laugh.

    “But seriously Mama,” Kate says once their laughter dies down, “I never thought I would have parents like this after my Dad passed away, and not only did I get you, but I got Mom too. I am so beyond grateful and thankful that I have both you and Mom in my life. And I would be so happy if you guys were to get married.”

    Natasha kisses Kate’s temple.

    “But you guys better remember to thank me in your vows,” Kate jokes, “if it weren’t for me, there wouldn’t be a wedding.”

    Natasha laughs, “Well I gotta get her to say yes first, malyshka.”

    “I wouldn’t worry about that,” Kate smiles, “How are you gonna ask her?”

    “I got some ideas,” Natasha smirks.

    A few days later, when the Quinjet touched down at the Compound, all Wanda could think about was a hot shower, some ice cream, and cuddling on the couch with her daughter and her girlfriend.

    However when she finally reaches the apartment, she finds it quiet and empty, except for Lucky who excitedly greets her at the door.

    “Hi Lucky,” she gives the happy dog some pats before calling out, “Hello? Nat? Kate?”

    “Hi Mom!” Kate smiles.

    “Hi bug!” Wanda smiles back and walks over to give Kate a hug, “why are you wearing your nice clothes?”

    “Oh this?” Kate looks down at her new slacks and nice button-down shirt, and then sends an easy grin back to Wanda, “no reason! But I am under specific orders to bring you to the roof.”

    “The roof?” Wanda asks, “why?”

    Kate just smiles and shrugs, “I dunno.”

    “Hmm,” Wanda hums non-committedly, looking Kate up and down skeptically.

    Kate just offers Wanda her arm, and Wanda rolls her eyes and takes it.

    “You are not taking me up there to kill me, right?” Wanda jokes.

    “Come on, Mom,” Kate chuckles as she leads Wanda to the roof access.

    Once they reach the roof, Kate opens the door, and Wanda pauses in awe.

    The whole dome is strung with little, warm fairy lights and there is a path lined with candles to the middle of the room, where Natasha is waiting.

    Wanda looks down at Kate with tears already in her eyes, and Kate smiles up at her. Kate pulls out a rose that she had hidden in her pocket and hands it to Wanda. Wanda kisses Kate on the temple, and makes her way to Natasha.

    “Hey moya lyubov,” Natasha greets her and pulls her into her arms.

    “What is all this?” Wanda asks, still slightly in awe.

    “Well, according to our daughter,” Natasha chuckles lightly while sending a smile back to Kate, “it is something I should’ve done a long time ago.”

    Wanda chuckles “This year has changed me in ways I have never thought I was capable of. It has proved to me that I am so much more than what the Red Room has created me to be. I proved them wrong. I finally went against them, and took them down for good. I became a mother, another thing I thought was previously impossible, to the most amazing kid anyone would have asked for. And most of all, I think I finally believe that it is okay that I am not made of marble. That I am capable of love.

    And I owe a lot of that to you. Without you, I wouldn’t have felt confident enough to take them down. I wouldn’t be the mother I am without you. I certainly wouldn’t be the person I am today without you.

    I didn’t realize how much I was missing in my life without you in it. And I know, for sure, that I definitely would never want to live a life that you are not in.”

    Natasha gets down on one knee. The tears that have been building in Wanda’s eyes break free down her face.

    “Wanda, I love you with my whole heart,” Natasha puts her hand in her pocket and pulls out a beautiful hexagon-cut moss agate ring on a gold band, “will you marry me?”

    Wanda immediately nods her head yes as she leans down to kiss Natasha. Natasha meets her halfway and stands up to kiss her fully.

    “Is that a yes?” Natasha leans back to ask playfully, a few tears running down her face.

    “Of course it is a yes,” Wanda chuckles, “I love you, detka.”

    They kiss again before Natasha and Wanda break apart, and Natasha puts the ring on Wanda’s finger. Kate, who had begun to tear up once Natasha started talking, starts to cheer. Natasha and Wanda laugh as Kate sprints over to them and launches into their awaiting arms.

    “I am so happy,” Kate says softly, with a few tears still on her cheeks, as her parents hug her tight.

    Natasha reaches down and wipes them away, “Me too.”

    “This is all so beautiful,” Wanda says, looking around the room and back down at her ring, “how long did this take you guys?”

    “Ah, it was nothing,” Natasha shrugs off, “although, we may or may not have accidently superglued these lights to the dome so if Stark asks, we didn’t do it.”

    Wanda laughs, “Your secret is safe with me.”

    After a few comfortable moments of silence, Kate speaks up, “So… what do you guys wanna do now?”

    Natasha and Wanda chuckle.

    “I will leave that up to your Mom,” Natasha says, “She did have a long mission this week.” “And I know just how I want to celebrate,” Wanda replies with a smile.

    An hour later, Wanda is exactly where she wanted to be: cuddled on the couch with her daughter and her fiancée, watching some wholesome movie while they all share a tub of ice cream.

    Driving Craziness Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Throughout the following week, Kate (with the help of Yelena) planned a surprise engagement party for her parents for Saturday night. Kate just planned for it to be in the Common room and got all their friends and family to come.

    “I really don’t know, Mama,” Kate feels her palms start to sweat, “it is just what he told me.” “Uh-huh,” Natasha hums raising her eyebrow, definitely not believing Kate.

    Kate shrugs again and continues to walk to the Common room, picking up her pace.

    If I knew that this would involve lying to Mama, maybe I would’ve roped her into my plans. I HATE lying to Mama.

    Thankfully, they reach the Common Room doors not a moment later. They stop briefly outside, Natasha and Wanda sending each other confused looks when they notice none of the lights are on.

    “Love you too, milaya,” Wanda smiles, “Goodnight.”

    They both get up and linger by the door, waiting to watch Kate fall into a deeper sleep before they head to their room for the night.

    The next day at school, Kate shares the news with her friends. They are all ecstatic and can’t wait for the upcoming wedding.

    “Are we gonna be invited?” Greer asks excitedly, “I mean it will be totally understandable if we aren’t but it would be AMAZING!”

    Kate laughs.

    “Yeah!” Franny adds, “And I should have my license by then and would be able to drive us!” “Oh my god! That would be awesome!” Greer cheers.

    “Okay, okay!” Kate chuckles, “I will definitely try to get you guys on the list. But no promises!”

    “Sweet!” Greer fist pumps.

    Both Franny and Kate laugh. After they calm down Kate says, “I didn’t know you were close to getting your license, Franny?”

    “Yeah!” Franny smiles, “I should have it in a few months! I can take my test the day I turn 16!”

    “Wow! That’s awesome!” Kate says, “With all the craziness of my life this past year, I haven’t even practiced yet. I got my permit a while ago when I took drivers ed before I started living with Tasha, but I have NEVER been behind the wheel.”

    “Well now is the perfect time to start!” Greer encourages, “Archery doesn’t start til after the new year, and you have plenty of time before you turn 16 to practice!”

    “You are right!” Kate smiles, “I will talk to Mama after school today!”

    “Maybe she will even teach you some cool driving tricks! Like driving backwards or going off ramps and stuff!” Greer excitedly lists.

    Kate chuckles and shakes her fondly.

    “Hey malyshka,” Natasha greets Kate as she gets into the car, “How was school?”

    “It was school,” Kate shrugs, “but it wasn’t terrible.”

    “I guess that is good,” Natasha chuckles.

    Natasha pulls out of the school parking lot and starts to drive towards the Compound. After a few minutes, Natasha looks over to Kate slightly confused. Kate usually talks her ear off about her day or asks questions about what they are going to do that evening, but today she is being uncharacteristically quiet.

    “You okay, malen’kiy yastreb?” she asks.

    “Yeah, I am okay,” Kate replies quickly then starts to fidget, “actually, Mama, I have a question.”

    “What is it, dorogoy?”

    “Can… can you teach me how to drive? I know it seems early but I am gonna be 16 next year and I should probably get my license and I don’t wanna bother you and Mom for rides all the time and–”

    “Woah woah, calm down Katie,” Natasha chuckles, “I think it’s a great idea!”

    “Really?” Kate softly smiles.

    “Yeah!” Natasha replies, “As much as I hate that you are getting older and I can’t freeze you at this age, I will acknowledge the fact that you need to learn new things.”

    “Wow,” Kate chuckles softly, “I kinda thought I would need to convince you more.”

    Natasha shrugs, then smiles conspiratorially, “do you wanna learn now?”

    Cut to a very nervous Kate sitting in the driver’s seat of Natasha’s Corvette in an empty parking lot with Natasha in the passenger’s seat.

    “Okay, Katie, now shift the car into drive,” Natasha instructs.

    “Um okay,” Kate goes to shift the car into gear, but then pauses and looks directly into Natasha’s eyes, “Are you sure this is okay?”

    “Of course, malyshka,” Natasha reassures, “now let’s get movin!”

    Kate slowly shifts into gear and looks to Natasha for her next instruction.

    “Okay, now slowly edge your foot off of the break and move it over to the gas,” Natasha says.

    Kate nods, moves her foot and slightly presses on the gas. She squeals lightly when the car starts to move. Natasha chuckles.

    After an hour or so of driving, Natasha tells Kate to pull out of the parking lot.

    “Wait, what?” Kate asks, confused, “What do you mean, pull out of the parking lot?

    Shouldn’t we switch spots?”

    “No,” Natasha smiles confidently, “You are gonna drive us home.”

    “ME?!”

    “Yes, you,” Natasha chuckles.

    “But—but—”

    “No buts, malyshka,” Natasha cuts her off, “I know it will be a little scary, but I will be here the whole time.”

    “Okay,” Kate replies, hesitantly, before turning out of the parking lot.

    Most of the ride home was uneventful, outside of some abrupt breaking and some jerk-y turns that comes along with a beginner driver. But as they reached about the halfway point, Kate notices Natasha’s attention has shifted from instructing Kate to intermittently checking the rearview mirror; in more than just procedural way.

    “Kate, take this right,” Natasha’s voice comes out more serious and determined as it had been.

    “But that is not the way home,” Kate points out.

    “Humor me,” Natasha replies, not giving any other clues as to why there is a sudden shift in their usual route.

    “Okay,” Kate shrugs and takes the turn.

    Natasha’s focus becomes fixated on the rearview mirror yet again. Kate watches her type something rapidly into her phone before her gaze locks on to the rearview mirror again.

    Confused, Kate also looks in the rearview mirror briefly, but all she sees are a few cars behinds her.

    “Take this left,” Natasha instructs.

    “Are we just doing this for fun?” Kate chuckles slightly, “Because I kinda thought we would be going home. I do have homework you know—”

    “Just listen to me,” Natasha’s tone becomes stricter and more urgent.

    Kate deflates slightly, “Okay, I was just joking.”

    “I know dorogoy,” Natasha sighs, “I am sorry.”

    It is then Kate notices Natasha’s slight concern, “What’s wrong, Mama?”

    “I need you to stay calm, okay?” Natasha says in a gentle, measured voice, “That black truck behind us has been following us since we left the parking lot. And out of caution, I ran its plates, and they came back stolen.”

    Kate’s heartrate immediately picks up.

    “Oh-okay,” Kate starts to tremble slightly, “so what do we do? Should we switch?”

    “Unfortunately, I don’t think we have the opportunity to do so right now,” Natasha explains, “If we stop, they could also stop and that could lead to a dangerous situation. Our best bet right now is to try and lose them.”

    “And how do we do that?”

    “We continue to drive safely, taking random turns, until they get bored and drive off, hopefully.”

    “What happens if they don’t drive off?”

    “Well,” Natasha leans back in her seat, “we will cross that bridge if or when we come to it.” “Great,” Kate tries to take some deep breaths, “this is great.”

    Natasha reaches over and squeezes Kate’s shoulder, “We got this, malen’kiy yastreb. We will be okay.”

    A few turns and stoplights later, the truck is still behind them. And now it is directly behind them, not just a few cars away. Kate has since stopped trying to regulate her breathing, knowing fully well that she is starting to panic.

    “You are doing great, baby,” Natasha encourages, “I promise they will back off soon.”

    Almost as soon those words leave Natasha’s mouth, a gunshot rings out and a bullet lodges itself in the corvette’s back windshield.

    Thank god for bulletproof glass.

    “Drive!” Natasha instructs Kate and Kate presses steadily on the gas.

    “Where am I going?!?” Kate shouts panickily to Natasha, who is currently pulling her gun out of her glove box.

    “Just go straight and keep your foot on the gas,” Natasha instructs as more gunshots continue to sound, “I am pretty sure this road leads to some farm land. Less collateral damage.”

    “Okay—okay,” Kate shakily grips the steering wheel until her knuckles turn white.

    “If you need to, go around the other cars ahead of you,” Natasha says, rolling down her window, “Just be careful.”

    “What are you doing?!?” Kate asks incredulously.

    “Taking care of this,” Natasha replies simply, as she leans out the window and begins to fire at the tires of the car behind them.

    Kate feels her panic hit a fever pitch when her Mama sticks most of her body out the window. She tries to take a deep breath and focus on the road ahead of her, having to quickly swerve around the cars that are in front of her. She can’t help the few tears that run down her cheeks. Every once in a while, Natasha would duck down and dodge a bullet or two, making Kate’s heart feel like it was going to beat out of her chest.

    After what feels like hours, Natasha manages to finally shoot out both front tires, and the truck careens off the road into an empty farm field. She instructs Kate to slow down and pull over.

    “You did great, malen'kiy yastreb,” Natasha soothes Kate, “now I have to go deal with these douchebags. Stay here, okay?”

    Natasha notices that Kate has not moved since she pulled over; her hands are still tightly gripping the steering wheel and she is still staring off through the windshield.

    “Kate? You okay, baby?” Natasha asks.

    She grows concerned when she still doesn’t get a response, but out of the corner of her eye, she can see said douchebags starting to exit the totaled truck.

    “Okay,” Natasha sighs, smiling softly towards Kate, talking to her as if she had woken up from a bad dream, “I need to go handle these guys until the police get here and can take over. It shouldn’t be long baby, okay?”

    Still no response. Natasha frowns.

    “We are okay now, baby,” she kisses Kate’s temple, “we are safe.”

    She takes one last look at Kate before taking the keys out of the ignition, just in case, and going outside to teach those dickheads a lesson.

    Thankfully the police show up less than 15 minutes later to haul them away, and Natasha returns to the car. She walks to the driver’s side and opens the door. Kate is in the same position, but instead of her muscles being rigid like they were before, they seem to be more relaxed.

    “Malyshka?”

    Kate slowly turns to look at Natasha, and as soon as their eyes lock, she lets out a soft sob as tears start to race down her face.

    “Oh baby, come here,” Natasha leans down and pulls Kate into her arms, “You did such a good job malyshka. We are safe. We are safe.”

    Kate pushes her head into Natasha’s neck, and they stay like that for a while, until Kate’s tears slow. Natasha pulls back and wipes the tears off of Kate’s cheeks.

    “Wanna go home?” Natasha asks softly.

    Kate immediately nods, and quickly makes her way over to the passenger seat.

    Natasha called Wanda when she was waiting for the police to arrive to let her know what had happened, and how shaken up Kate was. So, needless to say, Wanda was all prepared for the rest of the afternoon and evening when they came home.

    As soon as they walk into the apartment, Wanda holds her arms open, and Kate runs into them. Wanda squeezes her tight before suggesting she go put on some comfy clothes. Once Kate goes into her room, Wanda gives Natasha a brief kiss and they head into the kitchen to plate up dinner.

    “So did you figure out who those guys worked for?” Wanda asks.

    “Not yet,” Natasha sighs as she wipes her hand down her face, “I am gonna see if Steve or Buck wanna come with me tomorrow to interrogate them. It seems like they were just some guys that got in over their heads in a deal gone bad, because honestly, when I got out of the car, they were almost shocked that it was me.”

    “You mean you think they thought your car was someone else’s?”

    Natasha shrugs, “I think so, but I am not 100% sure. We will see tomorrow.”

    They finish plating the food and bring it over to the couch. Wanda had decided a couch picnic was called for after the crazy afternoon Natasha and Kate had.

    “She seems a little better than what you described earlier,” Wanda comments, looking towards Kate’s bedroom.

    “I think she is,” Natasha supplies, “she is no longer catatonic like she was right after it happened.”

    Wanda hums, but before she can speak her thought, Kate comes out of her room and sits right in between her and Natasha. Wanda hands her a bowl.

    “Here you go, milaya,” Wanda smiles softly, “I made your favorite.”

    “Thank you, Mom,” Kate starts to eat slowly.

    After they are done, Wanda takes the bowls and brings them into the kitchen. Natasha turns on a wholesome movie, and Kate immediately leans and puts her head on Natasha’s chest.

    Natasha tightens her arms around Kate then leans down and kisses her temple as Wanda comes back, sits next to Kate, and starts rubbing soothing circles onto Kate’s back.

    Kate lets out a deep sigh and melts further into her parents’ embraces.

    “I know today was scary and was a lot,” Natasha says softly, “but I am so proud of you, baby. You kept your cool under pressure, you kept us both safe, and not to mention, you drove really well.”

    Kate chuckles softly at the last bit.

    “Not even Clint has driven that well during a car chase before,” Wanda adds, “and he is supposed to be the one with the good eye for things.”

    Kate continues to chuckle, “well forgive me if I don’t wanna do that anytime soon.”

    “Oh, I don’t want you to have to do that ever again,” Natasha laughs lightly.

    “And I think I would like to hold off on practicing to driving for a while,” Kate concludes. “That is totally fair, bug,” Wanda agrees, chuckling as well, “and maybe next time we have you drive the Subaru and not the ‘Vette.”

    Kate snuggles deeper into Natasha and pulls Wanda closer, her eyes starting to flutter. Wanda kisses Kate’s temple.

    “Go to sleep, baby,” Natasha murmurs, “we aren’t going anywhere.”

    “Love you,” Kate manages to slip out right before she falls asleep.

    “We love you too, milaya,” Wanda replies, “Goodnight.”
